# Contents

Contents
Parliament has resolved on this Act of the Czech Republic:
GENERAL PART
SCOPE OF CRIMINAL LAWS
No criminal offence without law
Prohibition of retroactivity
An act is punishable only if its punishability was established by law before it was committed.  
Temporal scope
Punishability of an act and time of its commission
(1) The punishability of an act shall be assessed according to the law in force at the time the act was committed; it shall be assessed according to a later law only if that is more favourable to the offender.  
(2) If the law changes during the commission of an act, the law that is in force at the completion of the conduct by which the act is committed shall apply.  
(3) In the event of subsequent changes to the law that is in force at the completion of the conduct by which the act is committed, the mildest law shall apply.  
(4) An act is committed at the time when the offender or participant acted or, in the case of omission, was obliged to act. It is not decisive when the consequence occurs or was to occur.  
Application of the law in force at the time of decision
(1) Only such type of punishment as is permitted by the law in force at the time the criminal offence is being decided may always be imposed on the offender.  
(2) A protective measure shall always be decided according to the law in force at the time the protective measure is being decided.  
Territorial scope
Principle of territoriality
(1) The punishability of an act committed on the territory of the Czech Republic shall be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic.  
(2) A criminal offence is deemed to have been committed on the territory of the Czech Republic if,
a) the offender committed the conduct wholly or partly there, even if the violation or endangerment of an interest protected by criminal law occurred or was to occur wholly or partly abroad, or
b) the offender violated or endangered an interest protected by criminal law there, or such consequence was to occur there at least partly, even if the conduct was committed abroad.  
(3) Participation is committed on the territory of the Czech Republic if,
a) the act of the offender is committed there, where the place of commission of such act is assessed analogously according to paragraph 2, or
b) the participant in an act committed abroad acted there partly.  
(4) If a participant acted on the territory of the Czech Republic, the law of the Czech Republic shall apply to participation regardless of whether the act of the offender is punishable abroad.  
Principle of registration
The punishability of an act committed outside the territory of the Czech Republic on board a ship or other vessel, or an aircraft or other air transport vehicle registered in the Czech Republic shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic. The place of commission of such act shall be assessed analogously according to § 4 paragraphs 2 and 3.  
Principle of personality
The punishability of an act committed abroad by a citizen of the Czech Republic or a stateless person who has been granted permanent residence on its territory shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic.  
Principle of protection and principle of universality
(1) The punishability of torture and other inhuman and cruel treatment (§ 149), counterfeiting and alteration of money (§ 233), uttering counterfeit and altered money (§ 235), production and possession of counterfeiting tools and equipment intended for unauthorised acquisition of a means of payment (§ 236), unauthorised production of money (§ 237), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311), terror (§ 312), participation in a terrorist group (§ 312a), financing of terrorism (§ 312d), support and promotion of terrorism (§ 312e), threatening with a terrorist criminal offence (§ 312f), sabotage (§ 314), espionage (§ 316), violence against a public authority (§ 323), violence against an official (§ 325), counterfeiting and alteration of a public instrument (§ 348), genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (§ 402), preparation of an aggressive war (§ 406), use of a prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat (§ 411), war cruelty (§ 412), persecution of the population (§ 413), pillage in the area of war operations (§ 414), misuse of internationally recognised and state emblems (§ 415), misuse of flag and truce (§ 416) and harm to a member of parliament (§ 417) shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if such criminal offence was committed abroad by a foreign national or a stateless person who has not been granted permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic.  
(2) The punishability of an act committed abroad against a citizen of the Czech Republic or against a stateless person who has been granted permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic shall be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if the act is punishable at the place of commission of the act or if the place of commission of the act is not subject to any criminal jurisdiction.  
Subsidiary principle of universality
(1) The punishability of an act committed abroad by a foreign national or a stateless person who has not been granted permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if
a) the act is also punishable under the law in force in the territory where it was committed,
b) the offender was apprehended on the territory of the Czech Republic, extradition or surrender proceedings took place and the offender was not extradited or surrendered for criminal prosecution or execution of punishment to a foreign state or other authorised entity, and
c) the foreign state or other authorised entity that requested the extradition or surrender of the offender for criminal prosecution or execution of punishment has requested that criminal prosecution of the offender be conducted in the Czech Republic.  
(2) The punishability of an act committed abroad by a foreign national or a stateless person who has not been granted permanent residence on the territory of the Czech Republic shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if the act was committed for the benefit of a legal person that has its registered office or organisational unit on the territory of the Czech Republic.  
(3) However, no punishment more severe than that provided by the law of the state on whose territory the criminal offence was committed may be imposed on the offender.  
Scope established by international treaty
(1) The punishability of an act shall also be assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic if so provided by an international treaty that forms part of the legal order (hereinafter "international treaty").  
(2) The provisions of §§ 4 to 8 shall not apply if the international treaty does not permit it.

## § 10 repealed

Effects of a judgment of a foreign state
(1) A criminal judgment of a foreign state cannot be executed on the territory of the Czech Republic nor have any other effects there, unless provided otherwise by law or international treaty. (2) A final conviction by a court of another member state of the European Union in criminal proceedings shall be regarded for the purposes of criminal proceedings as a conviction by a court of the Czech Republic, if it was issued for an act that is also criminal under the law of the Czech Republic. CRIMINAL LIABILITY
Foundations of criminal liability
Principle of legality and principle of subsidiarity of criminal repression
(1) Only criminal law defines criminal offences and establishes criminal sanctions that may be imposed for their commission. (2) The criminal liability of the offender and the criminal law consequences connected therewith may be applied only in cases that are socially harmful, in which the application of liability under another legal regulation does not suffice. Criminal offence
(1) A criminal offence is an unlawful act that criminal law designates as criminal and that exhibits the characteristics specified in such law. (2) Criminal liability for a criminal offence requires intentional culpability, unless criminal law expressly provides that culpability by negligence suffices. Misdemeanours and felonies
(1) Criminal offences are divided into misdemeanours and felonies. (2) Misdemeanours are all negligent criminal offences and those intentional criminal offences for which criminal law establishes a sentence of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range up to five years. (3) Felonies are all criminal offences that are not misdemeanours under criminal law; particularly serious felonies are those intentional criminal offences for which criminal law establishes a sentence of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range of at least ten years. Culpability
Intent
(1) A criminal offence is committed intentionally if the offender
a) wanted to violate or endanger an interest protected by such law in the manner specified in criminal law, or
b) knew that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, and was reconciled to the possibility that he would cause it. (2) Reconciliation means also the offender's acceptance that he may violate or endanger an interest protected by such law in the manner specified in criminal law. Negligence
(1) A criminal offence is committed by negligence if the offender
a) knew that he could violate or endanger an interest protected by such law in the manner specified in criminal law, but relied without reasonable grounds that he would not cause such violation or endangerment, or
b) did not know that by his conduct he could cause such violation or endangerment, although he should and could have known this in view of the circumstances and his personal situation. (2) A criminal offence is committed by gross negligence if the offender's approach to the requirement of due care demonstrates the offender's obvious disregard for interests protected by criminal law. Culpability as to a particularly aggravating circumstance
A circumstance that conditions the application of a higher sentencing range shall be taken into account if
a) it concerns a more serious consequence, even if the offender caused it by negligence, except in cases where criminal law requires intentional culpability even here, or
b) it concerns another fact, even if the offender did not know about it, although he should and could have known about it in view of the circumstances and his personal situation, except in cases where criminal law requires that the offender knew about it. Mistake of fact
(1) A person who, when committing an act, does not know and does not assume as possible a factual circumstance that is a characteristic of a criminal offence does not act intentionally; this does not affect liability for a criminal offence committed by negligence. (2) A person who, when committing an act, mistakenly assumes factual circumstances that would constitute the characteristics of a less serious intentional criminal offence shall be punished only for this less serious criminal offence, unless it is a criminal offence committed by negligence. (3) A person who, when committing an act, mistakenly assumes factual circumstances that would constitute the characteristics of a more serious intentional criminal offence shall be punished for an attempt of this more serious criminal offence. (4) A person who, when committing an act, mistakenly assumes a factual circumstance that excludes its unlawfulness does not act intentionally; this does not affect liability for a criminal offence committed by negligence. Mistake of law
(1) A person who, when committing a criminal offence, does not know that his act is unlawful does not act culpably if he could not have avoided the mistake. (2) The mistake could have been avoided if the duty to become familiar with the relevant legal regulation arose for the offender from law or another legal regulation, an official decision or contract, from his employment, profession, position or function, or if the offender could have recognised the unlawfulness of the act without obvious difficulties. Preparation for and attempt of a criminal offence
Preparation
(1) Conduct that consists in the intentional creation of conditions for the commission of a particularly serious felony (§ 14(3)), in particular in its organisation, procurement or adaptation of means or instruments for its commission, in association, assembly, in instigation or aiding of such a felony, is preparation only if criminal law expressly provides this for the relevant criminal offence and provided that there was no attempt or completion of the particularly serious felony. (2) Preparation is punishable according to the sentencing range established for the particularly serious felony to which it was directed, unless criminal law provides otherwise. (3) Criminal liability for preparation for a particularly serious felony ceases if the offender voluntarily desisted from further conduct directed at committing the particularly serious felony and
a) eliminated the danger that arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken preparation, or
b) made a report about the preparation for the particularly serious felony at a time when the danger that arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken preparation could still be eliminated; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. (4) If more persons are involved in the act, the cessation of criminal liability for preparation of an offender who so acted is not prevented if the act is completed by the other offenders independently of his earlier contribution to the act or despite his timely report. (5) The provisions of paragraphs 3 and 4 do not affect the criminal liability of the offender for another completed criminal offence that he already committed by the conduct specified in paragraph 1. Attempt
(1) Conduct that is directly aimed at the completion of a criminal offence and which the offender committed with the intent to commit the criminal offence is an attempt of a criminal offence if the criminal offence was not completed. (2) An attempt of a criminal offence is punishable according to the sentencing range established for the completed criminal offence. (3) Criminal liability for an attempt of a criminal offence ceases if the offender voluntarily desisted from further conduct directed at completing the criminal offence and
a) eliminated the danger that arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken attempt of the criminal offence, or
b) made a report about the attempt of the criminal offence at a time when the danger that arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken attempt of the criminal offence could still be eliminated; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. (4) If more persons are involved in the act, the cessation of criminal liability for an attempt of an offender who so acted is not prevented if the act is completed by the other offenders independently of his earlier contribution to the act or despite his timely report. (5) The provisions of paragraphs 3 and 4 do not affect the criminal liability of the offender for another completed criminal offence that he already committed by the conduct specified in paragraph 1. Perpetrator, co-perpetrator and participant in a criminal offence
Perpetrator
(1) The perpetrator of a criminal offence is a person who by his conduct fulfilled the characteristics of the factual elements of a criminal offence or its attempt or preparation, if it is punishable. (2) The perpetrator of a criminal offence is also a person who used another person to carry out the act, where that other person is not criminally liable due to lack of age, insanity, mistake, or because he acted in necessary defence, extreme necessity or under other circumstances excluding unlawfulness, or did not act at all or did not act culpably. The perpetrator of a criminal offence is also a person who used a person to carry out the act where that person did not act with a special intent or motive contemplated by law; in these cases the criminal liability of such person for another criminal offence that he committed by this conduct is not excluded. Co-perpetrator
If a criminal offence was committed by the intentional joint conduct of two or more persons, each of them is liable as if he had committed the criminal offence alone (co-perpetrators). Participant
(1) A participant in a completed criminal offence or its attempt is a person who intentionally
a) devised or directed the commission of the criminal offence (organiser),
b) induced in another the decision to commit the criminal offence (instigator), or
c) enabled or facilitated the commission of the criminal offence by another, in particular by procuring means, removing obstacles, luring the victim to the scene of the crime, keeping watch during the crime, giving advice, strengthening the resolve or promising to assist after the criminal offence (aider). (2) The provisions on the criminal liability and punishability of the perpetrator shall apply to the criminal liability and punishability of the participant, unless criminal law provides otherwise. (3) The criminal liability of a participant ceases if he voluntarily desisted from further participation in the criminal offence and
a) eliminated the danger that arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken participation, or
b) made a report about the participation in the criminal offence at a time when the danger that arose to an interest protected by criminal law from the undertaken participation could still be eliminated; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. (4) If more persons are involved in the act, the cessation of criminal liability of a participant who so acted is not prevented if the act is committed by the other perpetrators independently of his earlier contribution to the act or despite his timely report. (5) The provisions of paragraphs 3 and 4 do not apply to the criminal liability of a participant for another criminal offence that he already committed by the conduct specified in paragraph 1. Age
A person who at the time of committing the act had not reached the age of fifteen is not criminally liable. Insanity
A person who due to a mental disorder at the time of committing the act could not recognise its unlawfulness or control his conduct is not criminally liable for this act. Diminished sanity
A person who due to a mental disorder at the time of committing the act had substantially reduced capacity to recognise its unlawfulness or control his conduct is of diminished sanity. CIRCUMSTANCES EXCLUDING THE UNLAWFULNESS OF AN ACT
Extreme necessity
(1) An otherwise criminal act by which someone averts a danger directly threatening an interest protected by criminal law is not a criminal offence. (2) It is not extreme necessity if it was possible to avert this danger under the given circumstances in another way, or the consequence caused is manifestly equally serious or even more serious than the one that was threatened, or the person to whom the danger was threatening was obliged to endure it. Necessary defence
(1) An otherwise criminal act by which someone averts a directly threatening or continuing attack on an interest protected by criminal law is not a criminal offence. (2) It is not necessary defence if the defence was entirely manifestly disproportionate to the manner of attack. Consent of the injured party
(1) A person does not commit a criminal offence if he acts on the basis of the consent of a person whose interests, about which this person may decide without restriction lawfully, are affected by the act. (2) Consent under paragraph 1 must be given in advance or simultaneously with the conduct of the person committing the otherwise criminal act, voluntarily, definitely, seriously and comprehensibly; if such consent is given only after the commission of the act, the offender is not punishable if he could reasonably assume that the person specified in paragraph 1 would otherwise have given this consent in view of the circumstances of the case and his situation. (3) With the exception of cases of consent to medical interventions that are at the time of the act in accordance with the legal order and the knowledge of medical science and practice, consent under paragraph 1 cannot be considered to include consent to bodily harm or killing. Permissible risk
(1) A person does not commit a criminal offence if, in accordance with the achieved state of knowledge and information that he had at the time of his decision on further procedure, he carries out within the framework of his employment, profession, position or function a socially beneficial activity by which he endangers or violates an interest protected by criminal law, if the socially beneficial result cannot be achieved otherwise. (2) It is not permissible risk if such activity endangers the life or health of a person without his consent given in accordance with another legal regulation, or the result to which it is directed manifestly does not correspond to the degree of risk, or the performance of this activity manifestly contradicts the requirements of another legal regulation, the public interest, the principles of humanity or is contrary to good morals. Authorised use of a weapon
A person does not commit a criminal offence if he uses a weapon within the limits established by another legal regulation. CESSATION OF CRIMINAL LIABILITY
Cessation of criminal liability by effective remorse
Effective remorse
Criminal liability for the criminal offences of failure to provide assistance (§ 150), failure to provide assistance by the driver of a vehicle (§ 151), spreading a contagious human disease (§ 152), spreading a contagious human disease by negligence (§ 153), endangering health by defective food and other items (§ 156), endangering health by defective food and other items by negligence (§ 157), entrusting a child to the power of another (§ 169), abduction (§ 172), hostage taking (§ 174), abandonment of a child or entrusted person (§ 195), breach of duty in the administration of another's property (§ 220), breach of duty in the administration of another's property by negligence (§ 221), breach of duty in insolvency proceedings (§ 225), breach of duty to make a truthful declaration of property (§ 227), damage to another's property (§ 228), reduction of tax, fee and similar mandatory payment (§ 240), failure to fulfil the notification obligation in tax proceedings (§ 243), endangering foreign exchange management (§ 247), breach of regulations on the circulation of goods in dealings with foreign countries (§ 261), breach of regulations on control of export of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 262), breach of obligations in the export of dual-use goods and technologies (§ 263), conducting foreign trade in military material without a permit or licence (§ 265), breach of duty in connection with the issuance of a permit and licence for foreign trade in military material (§ 266), general endangerment (§ 272), general endangerment by negligence (§ 273), breach of duty in threatening distress (§ 275), damage to and endangerment of the operation of a generally beneficial facility (§ 276), damage to and endangerment of the operation of a generally beneficial negligence (§ 294), damage to forest (§ 295), unauthorised handling of waste (§ 298), unauthorised handling of protected wild animals and wild-growing plants (§ 299), unauthorised handling of protected wild animals and wild-growing plants by negligence (§ 300), spreading a contagious disease of animals (§ 306), spreading a contagious disease and pest of useful plants (§ 307), high treason representation of the state and international organisation (§ 315), espionage (§ 316), endangering classified information (§ 317), endangering classified information by negligence (§ 318), participation in a non-state armed group aimed at operating in an armed conflict (§ 321a), mutiny of prisoners (§ 344), false accusation (§ 345), false testimony and false expert opinion (§ 346), false interpretation (§ 347), obstruction of justice (§ 347a), spreading an alarming message (§ 357), failure to prevent a criminal offence (§ 367), failure to report a criminal offence (§ 368) ceases if the offender voluntarily
a) prevented or remedied the harmful consequence of the criminal offence, or
b) made a report about the criminal offence at a time when the harmful consequence of the criminal offence could still be prevented; the report must be made to a public prosecutor or police authority; a soldier may instead make the report to a superior. Limitation of criminal liability
Limitation period
(1) Criminal liability for a criminal offence ceases upon the expiry of the limitation period, which is
a) thirty years, if it concerns a criminal offence for which criminal law permits the imposition of an exceptional sentence, and a criminal offence committed in the preparation or approval of a privatisation project under another legal regulation,
b) fifteen years, if the upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least ten years,
c) ten years, if the upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least five years,
d) five years, if the upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least three years,
e) three years for other criminal offences. (2) The limitation period begins to run for criminal offences where an effect is a characteristic or where an effect is a characteristic of a qualified factual element, from the moment when such effect occurred; for other criminal offences the limitation period begins to run from the termination of the conduct. For a participant the limitation period begins to run from the termination of the act of the principal perpetrator. (3) The limitation period does not include
a) the time during which it was not possible to bring the offender before a court due to a statutory obstacle,
b) the time during which criminal prosecution was suspended,
c) the time during which the victim of the criminal offence of serious bodily harm consisting in mutilation of sexual organs or sterilisation (§ 145), unlawful termination of pregnancy without the consent of the pregnant woman (§ 159), trafficking in human beings (§ 168), abduction committed for the purpose of forcing another into marriage (§ 172), extortion (§ 175) or oppression (§ 177) committed for the purpose of forcing another into marriage or to tolerate an intervention aimed at causing serious bodily harm consisting in mutilation of sexual organs, or one of the criminal offences specified in Chapter Three of the Special Part of this Act on criminal offences against human dignity in the sexual sphere, or the criminal offence of seduction to sexual intercourse (§ 202), was under eighteen years of age,
d) the probationary period of conditional suspension of criminal prosecution or conditional postponement of the filing of a motion for punishment,
e) the time during which it was not possible to criminally prosecute the offender in the Czech Republic, if it concerns an act whose criminality is assessed according to the law of the Czech Republic on the basis of § 8(1),
f) the time from the issuance of an arrest warrant until its revocation or until it loses its validity for another reason,
g) the time during which certain acts of criminal proceedings were temporarily dispensed with according to the Act on International Judicial Cooperation in Criminal Matters,
h) the time during which criminal prosecution was temporarily suspended. (4) The limitation period is interrupted by
a) the commencement of criminal prosecution for the criminal offence in question, as well as by the subsequent taking into custody, issuance of an arrest warrant, filing of a request for securing the extradition of a person from a foreign state, issuance of a European arrest warrant, filing of an indictment, motion for approval of an agreement on guilt and punishment, motion for punishment, pronouncement of a conviction judgment for this criminal offence or delivery of a penal order for such criminal offence to the accused, or
b) if the offender committed a new criminal offence during the limitation period for which criminal law establishes the same or more severe punishment. (5) Upon interruption of the limitation period a new limitation period begins. Exclusion from limitation
Criminal liability does not cease upon the expiry of the limitation period
a) for criminal offences specified in Chapter Thirteen of the Special Part of this Act, with the exception of the criminal offences of founding, supporting and promoting a movement aimed at suppressing human rights and freedoms (§ 403), expression of sympathy for a movement aimed at suppressing human rights and freedoms (§ 404), denial, questioning, approval and justification of genocide (§ 405), even if acts were committed in the past that would now have the characteristics of these criminal offences,
b) for the criminal offences of high treason (§ 309), subversion of the republic (§ 310), terrorist attack (§ 311) and terror (§ 312), if they were committed under such circumstances that they constitute a war crime or crime against humanity under the rules of international law,
c) for other criminal offences committed in the period from 25 February 1948 to 29 December 1989, for which the upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least ten years, if for reasons incompatible with the fundamental principles of the legal order of a democratic state there was no final conviction or acquittal, and which were committed by public officials or were committed in connection with the persecution of an individual or group of persons for political, racial or religious reasons. CRIMINAL SANCTIONS
Types of criminal sanctions and general principles for their imposition
Types of criminal sanctions
Criminal sanctions are punishments and protective measures. General provision for the imposition of criminal sanctions
(1) Criminal sanctions may be imposed only on the basis of criminal law. (2) Cruel and disproportionate criminal sanctions may not be imposed on the offender. The execution of a criminal sanction must not degrade human dignity. Proportionality of criminal sanctions
(1) A criminal sanction must be imposed with regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed and the circumstances of the offender. (2) Where the imposition of a criminal sanction that is less onerous for the offender suffices, a criminal sanction that is more severe for the offender must not be imposed. (3) When imposing criminal sanctions, regard shall also be had to the legally protected interests of persons injured by the criminal offence. Punishments
General principles for the imposition of punishments
Determination of the type and measure of punishment
(1) When determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall have regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence committed, to the personal, family, property and other circumstances of the offender and to his previous way of life and to the possibility of his reform; it shall further have regard to the offender's conduct after the act, in particular to his effort to compensate for the damage or eliminate other harmful consequences of the act; it shall also have regard to his attitude to the criminal offence in criminal proceedings, whether he concluded an agreement on guilt and punishment, declared his guilt or designated decisive facts as undisputed, and if he was designated as a cooperating accused, also to how significantly he contributed to the clarification of a felony committed by members of an organised group, in connection with an organised group or for the benefit of an organised criminal group. It shall also have regard to the effects and consequences that can be expected from the punishment for the future life of the offender. (2) The nature and seriousness of the criminal offence are determined in particular by the significance of the protected interest that was affected by the act, the manner of carrying out the act and its consequences, the circumstances under which the act was committed, the person of the offender, the degree of his culpability and his motive, intent or purpose. (3) When determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall have regard to mitigating and aggravating circumstances (§ 41 and 42), to the time that has elapsed since the commission of the criminal offence, to any change in the situation and to the length of the criminal proceedings if it lasted a disproportionately long time. When assessing the proportionality of the length of the criminal proceedings, the court shall have regard to the complexity of the case, to the procedure of the authorities active in criminal proceedings, to the significance of the criminal proceedings for the offender and to his conduct by which he contributed to delays in the criminal proceedings. (4) If the court does not impose a cumulative sentence or a joint sentence for continuation in a criminal offence, when determining the type of punishment and its measure it shall also have regard to the type and measure of punishments that were imposed on the offender for his other criminal activity and have not yet been executed, so that, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the criminal offence and the person of the offender, such punishment is not imposed that together with the punishments not yet executed would lead to a disproportionate punishment of the offender. (5) A circumstance that is a statutory characteristic of a criminal offence, including a circumstance that conditions the application of a higher sentencing range, may not be regarded as a mitigating or aggravating circumstance. A circumstance justifying an extraordinary reduction of the sentence of imprisonment may not be regarded as a mitigating circumstance. (6) An aggravating circumstance shall be taken into account if
a) it concerns a more serious consequence, even if the offender caused it by negligence, except in cases where criminal law requires intentional culpability even here,
b) it concerns another fact, even if the offender did not know about it, although he should and could have known about it in view of the circumstances and his personal situation, except in cases where criminal law requires that the offender knew about it. (7) When determining the type of punishment and its measure, the court shall have regard
a) in the case of co-perpetrators also to the extent to which the conduct of each of them contributed to the commission of the criminal offence,
b) in the case of an organiser, instigator and aider also to the significance and nature of their participation in the commission of the criminal offence,
c) in the case of preparation for a particularly serious felony and an attempt of a criminal offence also to the extent to which the offender's conduct approached the completion of the criminal offence, as well as to the circumstances and reasons why it was not completed. (8) If the offender obtained or attempted to obtain property benefit by the criminal offence, the court shall have regard to this when determining the type of punishment and its measure; if this is not excluded by his property or personal circumstances, it shall impose on him, having regard to the amount of such property benefit, one of the punishments that will affect him in property (§ 66 to 72), either as an independent punishment or in addition to another punishment. The court shall in particular consider the imposition of a pecuniary punishment (§ 67 to 69). Imposition of punishment on an offender of diminished sanity
(1) If the offender committed a criminal offence in a state of diminished sanity that he did not induce, not even by negligence, by the influence of an addictive substance, the court shall have regard to this circumstance when determining the type of punishment and its measure. (2) If the court considers that, having regard to the health condition of the offender specified in paragraph 1, it would be possible, while simultaneously imposing protective treatment (§ 99), to achieve the possibility of his reform even with a punishment of shorter duration, it shall reduce the sentence of imprisonment below the lower limit of the sentencing range, and it is not bound by the limitation specified in § 58(4), and shall simultaneously impose protective treatment. Mitigating circumstances
The court shall regard as a mitigating circumstance in particular that the offender
a) committed the criminal offence for the first time and under circumstances independent of him,
b) committed the criminal offence in strong agitation, out of compassion or due to lack of life experience,
c) committed the criminal offence under the pressure of dependence or subordination,
d) committed the criminal offence under the influence of a threat or coercion,
e) committed the criminal offence under the influence of difficult personal or family circumstances that he did not cause himself,
f) committed the criminal offence at an age close to the age of juveniles,
g) committed the criminal offence while averting an attack or other danger, although the conditions of necessary defence or extreme necessity were not fully met, or exceeded the limits of permissible risk or the limits of another circumstance excluding unlawfulness,
h) committed the criminal offence in a mistake of law that could have been avoided,
i) caused lower damage or other lesser harmful consequence by the criminal offence,
j) contributed to the elimination of the harmful consequences of the criminal offence or voluntarily compensated for the damage caused,
k) reported his criminal offence to the authorities himself,
l) confessed to the criminal offence,
m) assisted in clarifying his criminal activity or significantly contributed to the clarification of a criminal offence committed by another,
n) contributed, in particular as a cooperating accused, to the clarification of criminal activity committed by members of an organised group, in connection with an organised group or for the benefit of an organised criminal group,
o) sincerely regretted the criminal offence, or
p) led a proper life before committing the criminal offence. Aggravating circumstances
The court shall regard as an aggravating circumstance in particular that the offender
a) committed the criminal offence with deliberation or after prior consideration,
b) committed the criminal offence out of greed, revenge, national, racial, ethnic, religious, class or other similar hatred or out of another particularly reprehensible motive,
c) committed the criminal offence in a cruel or tormenting manner, insidiously, with particular cunning or in another similar manner,
d) committed the criminal offence by exploiting someone's need, distress, defencelessness, dependence or subordination,
e) violated a special duty by the criminal offence,
f) abused his employment, position or function to commit the criminal offence,
g) committed the criminal offence against a person participating in the rescue of life and health or in the protection of property,
h) committed the criminal offence against a child, a close person, a pregnant woman, a sick person, a disabled person, a person of advanced age or a helpless person and thereby endangered their life or health, caused them damage, injury to health or other harm or unjustly enriched himself at their expense,
i) induced another to an otherwise criminal act, offence or criminal offence, in particular a child under fifteen years of age, a juvenile or a person of an age close to the age of juveniles,
j) committed the criminal offence during a crisis situation, natural disaster or other event seriously threatening life, public order or property, or in a territory where evacuation is being or has been carried out,
k) caused higher damage or other greater harmful consequence by the criminal offence,
l) obtained higher benefit by the criminal offence,
m) committed the criminal offence on a larger scale, on more things or more persons, or committed the criminal offence or continued in it for a longer time,
n) committed more criminal offences,
o) committed the criminal offence with another person,
p) committed the criminal offence as an organiser, as a member of an organised group or member of an association, or
q) has already been convicted of a criminal offence; the court is entitled, according to the nature of the previous conviction, not to consider this circumstance as aggravating, in particular having regard to the significance of the protected interest that was affected by the act, the manner of carrying out the act and its consequences, the circumstances under which the act was committed, the person of the offender, the degree of his culpability, his motive and the time that has elapsed since the last conviction, and if it concerns an offender of a criminal offence that was committed in a state induced by a mental disorder, or an offender who indulges in the abuse of an addictive substance and committed the criminal offence under its influence or in connection with its abuse, also if he commenced treatment or took other necessary measures to commence it. Aggregate and cumulative sentence
(1) If the court convicts the offender of two or more criminal offences, it shall impose on him an aggregate sentence according to the provision that relates to the criminal offence that is most severely punishable among them; if it concerns a multiple-act concurrence of a larger number of criminal offences, the court may impose on the offender a sentence of imprisonment within the sentencing range whose upper limit is increased by one third; however, the upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment must not exceed twenty years even after this increase, and when imposing an exceptional sentence of imprisonment over twenty up to thirty years it must not exceed thirty years. In addition to the punishment admissible under such provision, another type of punishment may also be imposed within the framework of the aggregate sentence if its imposition would be justified by one of the criminal offences being tried. If the lower limits of the sentencing ranges of imprisonment are different, the lower limit of the aggregate sentence is the highest of them. If criminal law establishes for one of such criminal offences only a sentence of imprisonment, the aggregate sentence may only be a sentence of imprisonment as an independent sentence. (2) The court shall impose a cumulative sentence according to the principles specified in paragraph 1 when it convicts the offender of a criminal offence that he committed before a conviction judgment for another of his criminal offences was pronounced by the court of first instance. Together with the imposition of the cumulative sentence, the court shall annul the pronouncement on the punishment imposed on the offender by the earlier judgment, as well as all other decisions substantively following this pronouncement, insofar as they have lost their basis due to the change that occurred by this annulment. The cumulative sentence must not be more lenient than the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment. Within the framework of the cumulative sentence, the court must pronounce the punishment of loss of honorary titles or decorations, the punishment of loss of military rank, the punishment of forfeiture of property or the punishment of forfeiture of a thing if such punishment was already pronounced by the earlier judgment. (3) A conviction judgment under paragraph 2 means also a judgment by which, under the conditions of § 48(1), punishment was conditionally waived with supervision. Together with the imposition of the cumulative sentence, the court shall annul the pronouncement on the conditional waiver of punishment with supervision, as well as all other decisions substantively following this pronouncement, insofar as they have lost their basis due to the change that occurred by the annulment. (4) The provisions on cumulative sentence shall not apply if the earlier conviction is of such nature that the offender is regarded as if he had not been convicted, or if the earlier conviction judgment was issued by a court of another member state of the European Union. Waiver of imposition of cumulative sentence
The court shall waive the imposition of a cumulative sentence under § 43(2) if it considers that the punishment imposed by the earlier judgment is sufficient.# Imposition of Joint Sentence for Continuing Criminal Offence

(1) If the court convicts the perpetrator of a partial attack in a continuing criminal offence (§ 116), for whose other attacks he was convicted by a judgment that has already become final, the court shall annul in this earlier judgment the finding of guilt concerning the continuing criminal offence and the criminal offences committed in conjunction with it in a single act concurrence, the entire pronouncement on sentence, as well as other pronouncements that have their basis in the stated finding of guilt, and shall again, while being bound by the factual findings in the annulled judgment, decide on guilt for the continuing criminal offence, including the new partial attack, or criminal offences committed in conjunction with it in a single act concurrence, on a joint sentence for the continuing criminal offence, which must not be more lenient than the sentence imposed by the earlier judgment, and possibly also on consequential pronouncements that have their basis in the finding of guilt. Within the framework of the joint sentence for continuing criminal offence, the court must pronounce a sentence of loss of honorary titles or decorations, loss of military rank, forfeiture of property or forfeiture of a thing, if such a sentence was already pronounced by the earlier judgment.

(2) The provisions of § 43 and 44 shall be applied mutatis mutandis in cases where a sentence is being imposed simultaneously for multiple criminal offences.

(3) The provisions on joint sentence for continuing criminal offence shall also apply when the earlier conviction is of such a nature that the perpetrator is regarded as if he had not been convicted.

(4) The provisions on joint sentence for continuing criminal offence shall not apply if the earlier convicting judgment was issued by a court of another Member State of the European Union.

# Waiver of Punishment

## General Provision

(1) Punishment may be waived for a perpetrator who has committed a misdemeanour, regrets its commission and demonstrates effective efforts towards reform, if, having regard to the nature and seriousness of the misdemeanour committed and to the perpetrator's previous life, it can be reasonably expected that the mere adjudication of the matter will suffice for his reform and for the protection of society.

(2) The court shall waive punishment of a perpetrator designated as a cooperating accused if the conditions set out in § 178a paragraphs 1 and 2 of the Criminal Procedure Code are met and if the cooperating accused has given both in the preparatory proceedings and in the proceedings before the court a complete and truthful testimony about facts that are capable of significantly contributing to the clarification of a felony committed by members of an organised group, in connection with an organised group or for the benefit of an organised criminal group; the provision of § 48 paragraph 1 is not affected thereby. Punishment of a perpetrator designated as a cooperating accused may not be waived in this manner if the criminal offence committed by him is more serious than the felony to whose clarification he contributed, if he participated as an organiser or instigator in the commission of the felony to whose clarification he contributed, if he intentionally caused serious bodily harm or death, or if there are grounds for extraordinary increase of the sentence of imprisonment (§ 59).

(3) The court may also waive punishment if the perpetrator of preparation or attempt of a criminal offence did not recognise that the preparation or attempt, having regard to the nature or type of the object of attack on which the act was to be committed, or having regard to the nature or type of means by which the act was to be committed, could not lead to completion.

(4) If the court has waived punishment, the perpetrator is regarded as if he had not been convicted.

## Waiver of Punishment with Concurrent Imposition of Protective Treatment or Preventive Detention

(1) The court may also waive punishment if the perpetrator committed a criminal offence in a state of diminished responsibility or in a state induced by a mental disorder, and the court considers that protective treatment (§ 99), which it simultaneously imposes, will ensure the perpetrator's reform and the protection of society better than punishment. This provision shall not apply if the perpetrator induced the state of diminished responsibility or mental disorder, even through negligence, by the influence of an addictive substance.

(2) The court may also waive punishment if the perpetrator committed a felony in a state of diminished responsibility or in a state induced by a mental disorder, and it cannot be expected that the imposed protective treatment, having regard to the nature of the mental disorder and the possibilities of influencing the perpetrator, would lead to sufficient protection of society, and the court considers that preventive detention (§ 100), which it simultaneously imposes on the perpetrator, will ensure the protection of society better than punishment.

## Conditional Waiver of Punishment with Supervision

(1) Under the conditions set out in § 46 paragraphs 1, 2 or 3, the court may conditionally waive punishment and establish supervision over the perpetrator if it considers it necessary to monitor the perpetrator's behaviour for a specified period.

(2) In conditional waiver of punishment, the court shall establish a probationary period of up to one year and shall simultaneously impose supervision on the perpetrator (§ 49 to 51).

(3) The court may impose reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations on the perpetrator whose punishment has been conditionally waived, aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to compensate, according to his means, for the damage or to redress the non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence.

(4) The court may impose as reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations in particular:
a) to undergo training to acquire suitable work qualifications,
b) to undergo a suitable programme of social training and re-education,
c) to undergo treatment for addiction to addictive substances that is not protective treatment under this Act,
d) to undergo suitable psychological counselling programmes,
e) to refrain from visiting unsuitable environments, sporting, cultural and other social events and from contact with certain persons,
f) to refrain from unauthorised interference with the rights or legally protected interests of other persons,
g) to refrain from gambling, playing gaming machines and betting,
h) to refrain from consuming alcoholic beverages or other addictive substances,
i) to pay maintenance owed or another amount owed,
j) to apologise personally and publicly to the injured party, or
k) to provide reasonable satisfaction to the injured party.

(5) In the case of a perpetrator of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in paragraph 4, also some of the educational measures referred to in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions established for juveniles.

(6) If the perpetrator whose punishment has been conditionally waived leads a proper life during the probationary period and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may cancel the imposed reasonable restriction, reasonable obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them, or may cancel the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and control his behaviour to an increased extent; the court may make this decision no earlier than after the expiry of six months of the imposed probationary period. The established probationary period remains unaffected by the decision according to the first sentence.

(7) If the perpetrator whose punishment has been conditionally waived has led a proper life during the probationary period and has complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide on the imposition of punishment, possibly already during the probationary period.

(8) If the court has not made a decision according to paragraph 7 within one year from the expiry of the probationary period, without the perpetrator whose punishment has been conditionally waived being at fault, it shall be deemed that he has proved himself.

(9) If it has been declared that the perpetrator whose punishment has been conditionally waived has proved himself, or if it is deemed that he has proved himself, he is regarded as if he had not been convicted.

# Supervision

## Concept and Purpose of Supervision

(1) Supervision means regular personal contact of the perpetrator with an officer of the Probation and Mediation Service (hereinafter "probation officer"), cooperation in creating and implementing a probation supervision plan and control of compliance with the conditions imposed on the perpetrator by the court or arising from the law.

(2) The purpose of supervision is:
a) monitoring and controlling the perpetrator's behaviour, thereby ensuring the protection of society and reducing the possibility of repetition of criminal activity,
b) professional guidance and assistance to the perpetrator with the aim of ensuring that he leads a proper life in the future.

(3) Supervision over the perpetrator is carried out by a probation officer.

## Obligations of the Perpetrator

The perpetrator on whom supervision has been imposed is obliged to:
a) cooperate with the probation officer in the manner determined by the probation officer and fulfil the probation supervision plan,
b) report to the probation officer within the time limits determined by the probation officer,
c) inform the probation officer of his residence, employment and sources of livelihood, compliance with reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations imposed by the court and other important circumstances for the execution of supervision determined by the probation officer,
d) allow the probation officer entry into the dwelling in which he is staying.

## Obligations and Powers of the Probation Officer

The probation officer is obliged to exercise supervision over the perpetrator in accordance with the created probation plan, to assist him in his affairs and to fulfil the instructions given by the presiding judge to the Probation and Mediation Service and which are aimed at the execution of supervision and at ensuring that the perpetrator leads a proper life. The probation officer is obliged to regularly update the probation plan having regard to the results of supervision and to the personal, family and other circumstances of the perpetrator.

# Types of Sentences and Exceptional Sentence

## Types of Sentences

(1) For criminal offences committed, the court may impose the following sentences:
a) imprisonment,
b) home confinement,
c) community service,
d) pecuniary penalty,
e) forfeiture of property,
f) prohibition of activity,
g) prohibition of residence,
h) prohibition of keeping and breeding animals,
i) prohibition of attending sporting events,
j) prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events,
k) loss of honorary titles or decorations,
l) loss of military rank,
m) expulsion,
n) forfeiture of a thing.

(2) Imprisonment means, unless the Criminal Code provides otherwise:
a) unconditional sentence of imprisonment,
b) conditional sentence of imprisonment,
c) conditional sentence of imprisonment with supervision.

(3) A special type of sentence of imprisonment is an exceptional sentence (§ 54).

## Imposition of Multiple Sentences Independently and Concurrently

(1) If the Criminal Code provides for several sentences for a particular criminal offence, each such sentence may be imposed independently or multiple such sentences may be imposed concurrently. In addition to the sentence provided by the Criminal Code for a particular criminal offence, other sentences referred to in § 52 may also be imposed. However, home confinement may not be imposed concurrently with imprisonment and community service, community service concurrently with imprisonment, pecuniary penalty concurrently with forfeiture of property, and prohibition of residence concurrently with expulsion.

(2) Home confinement, community service, pecuniary penalty, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events, expulsion and prohibition of residence may be imposed independently, even if the Criminal Code does not provide for such a sentence for a particular criminal offence.

## Exceptional Sentence

(1) An exceptional sentence means both a sentence of imprisonment of more than twenty up to thirty years and a sentence of life imprisonment. An exceptional sentence may be imposed only for a particularly serious felony where the Criminal Code permits it.

(2) A sentence of imprisonment of more than twenty up to thirty years may be imposed by the court only if the seriousness of the particularly serious felony is very high or the possibility of the perpetrator's reform is particularly difficult.

(3) A sentence of life imprisonment may be imposed by the court only on a perpetrator who has committed a particularly serious felony of murder under § 140 paragraph 3, or who, in committing a particularly serious felony of public endangerment under § 272 paragraph 3, high treason (§ 309), terrorist attack under § 311 paragraph 3, terror (§ 312), genocide (§ 400), attack against humanity (§ 401), use of prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat under § 411 paragraph 3, war cruelty under § 412 paragraph 3, persecution of population under § 413 paragraph 3 or abuse of internationally recognised and state symbols under § 415 paragraph 3, intentionally caused the death of another person, provided that:
a) such particularly serious felony is extraordinarily serious having regard to a particularly reprehensible manner of execution of the act or to a particularly reprehensible motive or to a particularly severe and difficult to remedy consequence, and
b) the imposition of such a sentence is required for effective protection of society or there is no hope that the perpetrator could be reformed by a sentence of imprisonment of more than twenty up to thirty years.

(4) If the court imposes a sentence of life imprisonment, it may simultaneously decide that the period of execution of the sentence in a high-security prison shall not be counted towards the period of execution of the sentence for the purposes of conditional release.

# Imposition and Execution of Individual Sentences

## Imprisonment

(1) An unconditional sentence of imprisonment is imposed for a maximum of twenty years, unless it concerns an extraordinary increase of the sentence of imprisonment (§ 59), imposition of a sentence of imprisonment on a perpetrator of a criminal offence committed for the benefit of an organised criminal group (§ 108) or an exceptional sentence (§ 54).

(2) For criminal offences where the upper limit of the criminal rate of imprisonment does not exceed five years, an unconditional sentence of imprisonment may be imposed only on condition that, having regard to the person of the perpetrator, the imposition of another sentence would manifestly not lead to the perpetrator leading a proper life. For a criminal offence of neglect of mandatory maintenance under § 196 paragraph 1 or 2, an unconditional sentence of imprisonment may be imposed only on condition that the imposition of such a sentence is required for effective protection of society or there is no hope that the perpetrator could be reformed by another sentence.

(3) An unconditional sentence of imprisonment is executed pursuant to another legal regulation in prisons.

## Execution of Sentence of Imprisonment

(1) An unconditional sentence of imprisonment is executed in a differentiated manner in a prison:
a) with custody,
b) with high security.

(2) The court shall as a rule assign to a prison:
a) with custody a perpetrator who does not meet the conditions for assignment to a high-security prison,
b) with high security a perpetrator who has been imposed an exceptional sentence (§ 54), who has been imposed a sentence of imprisonment for a criminal offence committed for the benefit of an organised criminal group (§ 108), who has been imposed a sentence of imprisonment of at least eight years for a particularly serious felony (§ 14 paragraph 3), or who has been convicted of an intentional criminal offence and in the last five years has escaped or attempted to escape from custody, from execution of sentence or from execution of preventive detention.

(3) The court may assign the perpetrator to a prison of a different type than that to which he is to be assigned according to paragraph 2, if, having regard to the seriousness of the criminal offence and to the degree and nature of the perpetrator's disturbance, it considers that influencing him to lead a proper life will be better ensured in a different type of prison; however, it shall always assign to a high-security prison a perpetrator who has been imposed a sentence of life imprisonment.

(4) The manner of execution of sentence in individual types of prisons is regulated by another legal regulation.

## Transfer of Convicted Person to Prison of Different Type

(1) During the execution of a sentence of imprisonment, the court may decide on the transfer of the convicted person to a prison of a different type.

(2) The court shall decide on the transfer of the convicted person to a prison with custody if the behaviour of the convicted person and the manner in which he fulfils his obligations justify the conclusion that the transfer will contribute to his reform.

(3) The court may decide on the transfer of the convicted person to a high-security prison if:
a) the convicted person has seriously or repeatedly violated the established order or discipline, or
b) the convicted person has been found guilty by a final judgment of a criminal offence that he committed during the execution of sentence.

(4) From a high-security prison, the following may not be transferred:
a) a convicted person who has been imposed a sentence of life imprisonment and who has not yet served at least ten years of this sentence,
b) another convicted person who is serving a sentence in a high-security prison, before serving at least one quarter of the imposed sentence.

(5) On the motion of a convicted person who has continuously served at least one quarter of the imposed sentence in a high-security prison, but at least six months, the court may decide on his transfer to a prison with custody. A convicted person who has been imposed a sentence of life imprisonment may file a motion for transfer to a prison with custody no earlier than after serving ten years of this sentence.

(6) If the motion under paragraph 5 is not granted, the convicted person may repeat it only after the expiry of six months from the end of the proceedings on his previous motion.

## § 57revoked

Extraordinary Reduction of Imprisonment Sentence
(1) If the court considers, having regard to the circumstances of the case or to the circumstances of the offender, that the application of the imprisonment sentence prescribed by the criminal law would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that the offender's rehabilitation can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration, it may reduce the imprisonment sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range prescribed by this Act. (2) If the court considers that, having regard to the circumstances of the offender and the nature of the criminal activity committed by him, his rehabilitation can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration, it may reduce the imprisonment sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where it sentences an offender who
a) helped to prevent a criminal offence which another person was preparing or attempting, or
(3) If it can be considered that, having regard to the circumstances of the offender and the nature of the criminal activity committed by him, his rehabilitation can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration, an imprisonment sentence may be imposed below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where it is imposed by a judgment approving a plea agreement on guilt and sentence. (4) In reducing an imprisonment sentence pursuant to paragraphs 1 to 3, a sentence may not be imposed
a) below five years, if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least twelve years,
b) below three years, if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least eight years,
c) below one year, if the lower limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment is at least five years. (5) The court shall reduce the imprisonment sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range also for an offender designated as a cooperating accused, if the conditions set out in Section 178a(1) of the Code of Criminal Procedure are met and if the cooperating accused has given, both in the preparatory proceedings and in the proceedings before the court, a complete and truthful testimony about facts that are capable of significantly contributing to the clarification of a felony committed by members of an organised group, in connection with an organised group or for the benefit of an organised criminal group; in doing so, it shall take into account the nature of the criminal offence stated in his confession in comparison with the felony committed by members of an organised group, in connection with an organised group or for the benefit of an organised criminal group, to the clarification of which he contributed, further the significance of such conduct by him, the person of the offender and the circumstances of the case, in particular whether and in what manner he participated in such felony to the clarification of which he undertook, and what consequences he possibly caused by his conduct. It is not bound by the limitation set out in paragraph 4. (6) The court may reduce the imprisonment sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where it sentences an offender for preparation for a criminal offence or for an attempt to commit a criminal offence or for aiding and abetting a criminal offence and considers, having regard to the nature and gravity of the preparation or attempt or aiding and abetting, that the application of the imprisonment sentence prescribed by this Act would be disproportionately severe for the offender and that the offender's rehabilitation can be achieved even with a sentence of shorter duration. It is not bound by the limitation set out in paragraph 4. (7) The court may reduce the imprisonment sentence below the lower limit of the sentencing range also where the offender acted under a mistake of law but could have avoided such mistake (Section 19(2)), committed a criminal offence while averting an attack or other danger without the conditions of necessity (Section 28) or self-defence (Section 29) being fully met, or exceeded the limits of permissible risk (Section 31) or the limits of another circumstance excluding unlawfulness. It is not bound by the limitation set out in paragraph 4. Extraordinary Increase of Imprisonment Sentence
(1) For an offender who has again committed a particularly serious felony (Section 14(3)), although he has already been punished for such or another particularly serious felony, the court may impose a sentence in the upper half of the sentencing range of imprisonment prescribed in the criminal law, the upper limit of which is increased by one third, if the gravity of the particularly serious felony is high having regard to such recidivism and other circumstances of the case or if the possibility of the offender's rehabilitation is impaired. (2) The upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment may, after an increase pursuant to paragraph 1, exceed twenty years. When imposing an exceptional imprisonment sentence of more than twenty years up to thirty years, the upper limit must not exceed thirty years. House Arrest
(1) The court may impose a sentence of house arrest for up to two years if it sentences an offender for a misdemeanour, provided that
a) having regard to the nature and gravity of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, it can reasonably be considered that the imposition of this sentence, possibly in addition to another sentence, will suffice, and
b) the offender gives a written promise that during the specified period he will remain in the dwelling or part thereof at the specified address and will provide all necessary cooperation during the execution of control. (2) As an independent sentence, a sentence of house arrest may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of house arrest consists in the convicted person's obligation to remain during the execution of this sentence in the specified dwelling or part thereof during the time period determined by the court, unless prevented by important reasons, in particular the performance of employment or profession or the provision of health services by a health service provider as a result of his illness or injury; the health service provider is obliged to inform the criminal proceedings authority of this fact upon request. (4) The court shall determine the time period during which the convicted person is obliged to remain in the specified dwelling or part thereof, on working days, on days of rest from work and days off work, taking into account in particular his working hours and the time necessary to travel to work, to care for minor children and to attend to necessary personal and family matters, so as to appropriately restrict his liberty while ensuring all necessary needs of the convicted person and his family. The court may permit the convicted person to attend regular religious services or religious gatherings even on days of rest from work and days off work. (5) The court may impose supervision over the offender for the duration of the execution of the sentence of house arrest; Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. (6) The court may impose on the offender for the duration of the execution of this sentence reasonable restrictions or reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to make good, according to his means, the damage or to redress the non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender the unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (7) In the case of an offender of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures referred to in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions prescribed for juveniles. Revocation of Supervision or Reasonable Restrictions, Reasonable Obligations and Educational Measures
If a convicted person to whom a sentence of house arrest has been imposed leads a proper life and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed reasonable restriction, reasonable obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them, or may revoke the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and control his behaviour to an increased extent; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the execution of one third of the imposed sentence of house arrest, but at least after six months of the execution of this sentence. Substitute Imprisonment Sentence
(1) When imposing a sentence of house arrest, the court shall determine, for the event that the offender evades commencement of the execution of this sentence, breaches the agreed conditions of its execution without serious reason, culpably fails to execute the imposed sentence during the specified time period or otherwise frustrates the execution of this sentence or does not lead a proper life, a substitute imprisonment sentence of up to one year; the substitute imprisonment sentence must not exceed the upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment. The court may decide on the execution of the substitute imprisonment sentence even during the period specified for the execution of the sentence of house arrest; if it decides on the execution of the substitute imprisonment sentence, it shall also determine the manner of its execution. (2) If the convicted person has given cause for a decision that the substitute imprisonment sentence shall be executed after the execution of half of the imposed sentence of house arrest, the court shall reduce the length of the substitute imprisonment sentence determined pursuant to paragraph 1 by half. (3) Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, leave the sentence of house arrest in force even though the convicted person has given cause for a decision on the execution of the substitute imprisonment sentence, and
a) impose supervision over the convicted person for the duration of the execution of the sentence or its remainder,
b) extend the duration of the execution of this sentence by up to one year, provided that it does not exceed the maximum duration of this sentence prescribed in Section 60(1),
c) impose on the convicted person for the duration of the execution of the sentence or its remainder a reasonable restriction or reasonable obligation pursuant to Section 48(4) not yet imposed, aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life, or
d) impose on the convicted person for the duration of the execution of the sentence or its remainder some of the educational measures pursuant to Section 60(7), if he is of an age close to the age of juveniles. (4) Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. Community Service
(1) The court may impose a sentence of community service if it sentences an offender for a misdemeanour; as an independent sentence, a sentence of community service may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (2) The court shall as a rule not impose a sentence of community service in the case of an offender to whom a sentence of community service was converted into an imprisonment sentence pursuant to Section 65(3) during the three years preceding the imposition of this type of sentence. (3) A sentence of community service consists in the convicted person's obligation to perform work for public benefit purposes to the specified extent for the benefit of a provider of community service, which may be the state, a region, a municipality or a legal person engaged in education and science, culture, schooling, health protection, fire protection, environmental protection, support and protection of youth, animal protection, humanitarian, social, charitable, religious, physical education and sports activities and which carries out such activity for a public benefit purpose. The work must not serve the gainful purposes of the convicted person. Duration of Sentence of Community Service
(1) The court may impose a sentence of community service in the duration of 50 to 300 hours. (2) The court may impose on the offender for the duration of the sentence also reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to make good, according to his means, the damage or to redress the non-pecuniary harm caused by the misdemeanour, or to surrender the unjust enrichment obtained by the misdemeanour. (3) In the case of an offender of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures referred to in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions prescribed for juveniles. Position of the Offender and His Health Fitness
When imposing a sentence of community service, the court shall take into account the position of the offender, his state of health and the possibility of imposing this sentence. It shall not impose a sentence of community service if the offender is not fit for health reasons to perform work continuously. Execution of Sentence of Community Service
(1) The convicted person is obliged to perform community service personally and free of charge in his free time no later than one year from the day specified as the day of commencement of the execution of this sentence. This period does not include the time during which the convicted person
a) could not perform community service due to health or legal obstacles, or
b) was in custody or was serving an imprisonment sentence. (2) If a convicted person to whom a sentence of community service has been imposed leads a proper life and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed reasonable restriction, reasonable obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them; the court may make this decision at the earliest after six months from the day specified as the day of commencement of the execution of this sentence.
