(3) If the offender does not lead a proper life during the period from sentencing to the completion of the execution of the sentence of community service, evades commencement of the execution of the sentence, breaches the agreed conditions of the execution of the sentence of community service without serious reason, otherwise frustrates the execution of this sentence or culpably fails to execute this sentence within the specified period, the court may convert, even during the period specified for its execution, the sentence of community service or its remainder into an imprisonment sentence and decide at the same time on the manner of its execution; in doing so, each hour, even if only commenced, of the unexecuted sentence of community service shall count as one day of imprisonment. (4) Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, leave the sentence of community service in force or extend the duration of the execution of this sentence by up to six months, even though the convicted person has given cause for the conversion of the sentence pursuant to paragraph 3, and
a) impose supervision over the convicted person for the duration of the execution of the sentence or its remainder,
b) impose on the convicted person for the duration of the execution of the sentence or its remainder reasonable restrictions or reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4) not yet imposed, or
c) impose on the convicted person for the duration of the execution of the sentence or its remainder some of the educational measures pursuant to Section 63(3), if he is of an age close to the age of juveniles. Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the execution of supervision. (5) An offender to whom a sentence of community service has been imposed shall be regarded as not having been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed or the execution of the sentence or its remainder has been waived by a final decision. Forfeiture of Property
(1) The court may, having regard to the circumstances of the criminal offence committed and the circumstances of the offender, impose a sentence of forfeiture of property if it sentences the offender to an exceptional sentence or if it sentences him for a particularly serious felony by which the offender obtained or sought to obtain a pecuniary advantage for himself or for another. (2) Without the conditions of paragraph 1, the court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of property only if the criminal law permits the imposition of this sentence for the felony committed; as an independent sentence, a sentence of forfeiture of property may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) Forfeiture of property affects the entire property of the convicted person or that part of it which the court determines; however, forfeiture does not extend to funds or things which are necessary to satisfy the vital needs of the convicted person or of persons for whose maintenance or upbringing the convicted person is obliged to provide by law. (4) By a judgment on forfeiture of property, the community property of spouses is dissolved. (5) Forfeited property falls to the state. Liens on forfeited property do not cease to exist. Pecuniary Penalty
(1) The court may impose a pecuniary penalty if the offender has obtained or sought to obtain a pecuniary advantage for himself or for another by an intentional criminal offence. (2) Without the conditions of paragraph 1, the court may impose a pecuniary penalty only if
a) the criminal law permits the imposition of this sentence for the criminal offence committed, or
b) it imposes it for a misdemeanour and, having regard to the nature and gravity of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, it does not impose an unconditional imprisonment sentence at the same time. (3) As an independent sentence, a pecuniary penalty may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. Duration of Pecuniary Penalty
(1) A pecuniary penalty is imposed in daily rates and amounts to at least 20 and at most 730 whole daily rates. (2) A daily rate amounts to at least CZK 100 and at most CZK 50,000. (3) The court shall determine the number of daily rates with regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed. However, twice the number of daily rates must not, even together with the imposed imprisonment sentence and the substitute imprisonment sentence for a sentence of house arrest, exceed the upper limit of the sentencing range of imprisonment. The court shall determine the amount of one daily rate of the pecuniary penalty with regard to the personal and property circumstances of the offender. In doing so, it shall as a rule proceed from the net income which the offender has or could have on average per one day. (4) The offender's income, his property and the proceeds therefrom, as well as other bases for determining the amount of the daily rate, may be determined by the court's estimate. (5) The court shall state in the decision the number and amount of daily rates. Having regard to the personal and property circumstances of the offender, the court may determine that the pecuniary penalty shall be paid in reasonable monthly instalments; in doing so, it may determine that the advantage of instalments of the pecuniary penalty lapses if the offender fails to pay an instalment in time. (6) The court shall not impose a pecuniary penalty if it is evident that it would be uncollectible. Execution of Pecuniary Penalty
(1) Paid amounts of the pecuniary penalty fall to the state. (2) If the offender fails to pay the pecuniary penalty upon the court's summons or before the expiry of the period for which the execution of the sentence was postponed or for which instalments were permitted, and if it is evident that the enforcement of the pecuniary penalty could be frustrated or would be unsuccessful, the court shall convert the pecuniary penalty or its remainder into an imprisonment sentence and decide at the same time on the manner of its execution; in doing so, each completely unpaid amount corresponding to one daily rate shall count as two days of imprisonment. (3) An offender to whom a pecuniary penalty has been imposed for a criminal offence other than a particularly serious felony shall be regarded as not having been convicted as soon as the sentence has been executed or the execution of the sentence or its remainder has been waived by a final decision. Forfeiture of a Thing
(1) The court shall impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing which is a direct product of criminal activity. (2) The court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing
a) which is an instrument of criminal activity, or
b) which is an indirect product of criminal activity, if the value of the thing constituting a direct product of criminal activity is not negligible in relation to the value of the thing constituting an indirect product of criminal activity. (3) The court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing only if it is a thing belonging to the offender. (4) If the offender holds in breach of another legal regulation a thing referred to in paragraph 2, in relation to which forfeiture of the thing may be imposed, the court shall always impose this sentence on him as well. (5) Before the final effect of the decision, a prohibition on alienation of the forfeited thing applies, which also includes a prohibition on other dispositions aimed at frustrating the sentence of forfeiture of the thing. (6) A forfeited thing falls to the state. Liens on the forfeited thing do not cease to exist. Forfeiture of Substitute Value
(1) If the offender, before the imposition of the sentence of forfeiture of a thing, destroys, damages or otherwise devalues, alienates, renders unusable, removes or utilises, in particular consumes, a thing which the court could have declared forfeited pursuant to Section 70, or otherwise frustrates its forfeiture, the court may impose on him forfeiture of substitute value up to the amount corresponding to the value of such thing. The court may determine the value of the thing which the court could have declared forfeited on the basis of an expert statement or an expert opinion. (2) If a thing is, even if only in part, devalued, rendered unusable or removed, the court may impose forfeiture of substitute value in addition to forfeiture of the thing pursuant to Section 70. (3) Forfeited substitute value falls to the state. Liens on the forfeited substitute value do not cease to exist. Independent Sentence of Forfeiture of a Thing
The court may impose a sentence of forfeiture of a thing as an independent sentence only if the criminal law permits the imposition of this sentence and if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. Prohibition of Activity
(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of activity for one year to ten years if the offender committed a criminal offence in connection with this activity. (2) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of activity as an independent sentence only if the criminal law permits the imposition of this sentence for the criminal offence committed and if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of prohibition of activity consists in prohibiting the convicted person, during the execution of this sentence, from exercising a certain employment, profession or function or such activity for which a special permit is required, or the exercise of which is regulated by another legal regulation. Execution of Sentence of Prohibition of Activity
(1) The period of execution of the sentence of prohibition of activity does not include the period of execution of an imprisonment sentence; however, it does include the period during which the offender was, before the final effect of the judgment, deprived of the authorisation to carry out the activity which is the subject of the prohibition in connection with the criminal offence pursuant to another legal regulation or on the basis of a measure of a public authority, or was no longer permitted to carry out this activity or could not carry it out because he undertook to do so for the purposes of conditional deferral of the filing of a motion for punishment or conditional stay of criminal prosecution. (2) If the sentence of prohibition of activity has been executed, the offender shall be regarded as not having been convicted. Prohibition of Keeping and Breeding Animals
(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals for up to ten years if the offender committed a criminal offence in connection with keeping, breeding or caring for an animal. (2) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals as an independent sentence only if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals consists in prohibiting the convicted person, during the execution of this sentence, from keeping, breeding and caring for an animal. Execution of Sentence of Prohibition of Keeping and Breeding Animals
(1) The period of execution of the sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals does not include the period of execution of an imprisonment sentence; however, it does include the period during which the offender was, before the final effect of the judgment, on the basis of a decision or measure of a public authority, no longer permitted to keep or breed the animals which are the subject of the prohibition or to care for them, or could not keep or breed them or care for them because he undertook to do so for the purposes of conditional deferral of the filing of a motion for punishment or conditional stay of criminal prosecution. (2) If the sentence of prohibition of keeping and breeding animals has been executed, the offender shall be regarded as not having been convicted. Prohibition of Residence
(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of residence for one year to ten years for an intentional criminal offence if, having regard to the offender's previous way of life and the place of commission of the act, the protection of public order, the family, health, morality or property so requires; a sentence of prohibition of residence must not relate to the place or district in which the offender has permanent residence. (2) A sentence of prohibition of residence may be imposed as an independent sentence for a criminal offence for which the criminal law prescribes an imprisonment sentence the upper limit of the sentencing range of which does not exceed three years, if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) The court may impose on the offender for the duration of the execution of this sentence reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to make good, according to his means, the damage or to redress the non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender the unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (4) In the case of an offender of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures referred to in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions prescribed for juveniles. (5) The period of execution of an imprisonment sentence is not included in the period of execution of the sentence of prohibition of residence; the court shall make a decision pursuant to paragraphs 3 and 4 only after the execution of the imprisonment sentence or after conditional release. (6) If a convicted person to whom a sentence of prohibition of residence has been imposed leads a proper life during its execution and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed reasonable restriction, reasonable obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the execution of one third of the imposed sentence of prohibition of residence, but at least after six months of the execution of this sentence. (7) A sentence of prohibition of residence consists in the convicted person not being permitted, during the execution of this sentence, to stay in a specified place or in a specified district; a permit is required for temporary residence in such place or in such district in a necessary personal matter. Prohibition of Entry to Sporting, Cultural and Other Social Events
(1) The court may impose a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events for up to ten years if the offender committed an intentional criminal offence in connection with attendance at such an event. (2) As an independent sentence, a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the misdemeanour committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (3) A sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events consists in prohibiting the convicted person, during the execution of this sentence, from attending specified sporting, cultural and other social events. Execution of Sentence of Prohibition of Entry to Sporting, Cultural and Other Social Events
(1) During the execution of a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events, the convicted person is obliged to cooperate with the probation officer in the manner determined by him, in particular to proceed according to the established probation plan, to undergo established programmes of social training and re-education, programmes of psychological counselling, and, if the probation officer considers it necessary, to report according to his instructions in the period immediately connected with the holding of the prohibited event to a specified unit of the Police of the Czech Republic. (2) The period of execution of an imprisonment sentence is not included in the period of execution of the sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events. Loss of Honorary Titles or Decorations
(1) The court may impose a sentence of loss of honorary titles or decorations if it sentences the offender for an intentional criminal offence committed from a particularly reprehensible motive to an unconditional imprisonment sentence of at least two years. (2) Loss of honorary titles or decorations consists in the convicted person losing decorations, honorary recognitions and other honorary titles awarded pursuant to domestic legal regulations. Loss of Military Rank
(1) The court may impose a sentence of loss of military rank if it sentences the offender for an intentional criminal offence committed from a particularly reprehensible motive to an unconditional imprisonment sentence of at least two years. (2) The court may impose this sentence also in addition to another sentence if, having regard to the nature of the criminal offence committed, discipline and order in the armed forces so require. (3) Loss of military rank consists in the convicted person's rank in the armed forces being reduced to the rank of private. Expulsion
(1) The court may impose on an offender who is not a citizen of the Czech Republic a sentence of expulsion from the territory of the Czech Republic, either as an independent sentence or in addition to another sentence, if the safety of persons or property, or another general interest, so requires; as an independent sentence, a sentence of expulsion may be imposed if, having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed and the person and circumstances of the offender, the imposition of another sentence is not necessary. (2) Having regard to the nature and gravity of the criminal offence committed, the possibilities of rehabilitation and the circumstances of the offender and the degree of threat to the safety of persons, property or another general interest, the court may impose a sentence of expulsion in the duration of one year to ten years, or for an indefinite period. (3) The court shall not impose a sentence of expulsion if
a) it has not been possible to ascertain the offender's nationality,
b) the offender has been granted asylum pursuant to another legal regulation,
c) its consequence would be a disproportionate interference with the offender's private or family life,
d) there is a danger that the offender will be persecuted in the state to which he should be expelled for his race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, membership of a certain social group, political or religious beliefs, or if expulsion would expose the offender to torture or other inhuman or degrading treatment or punishment,
e) the offender is a citizen of the European Union or his family member regardless of nationality and has been granted permanent residence in the territory of the Czech Republic or is a foreigner with the recognised legal status of a long-term resident in the territory of the Czech Republic pursuant to another legal regulation, unless serious reasons of threat to state security or public order are found,
f) the offender is a citizen of the European Union and has continuously resided in the territory of the Czech Republic for the last 10 years, unless imperative reasons of threat to state security are found, or
g) the offender is a child who is a citizen of the European Union, unless expulsion would be in his best interests. Conditional Sentencing to Imprisonment
Conditional Suspension of Execution of Imprisonment Sentence
(1) The court may conditionally suspend the execution of an imprisonment sentence not exceeding three years if, having regard to the person and circumstances of the offender, in particular with regard to his previous life and the environment in which he lives and works, and to the circumstances of the case, it reasonably considers that its execution is not necessary to influence the offender to lead a proper life. (2) The permission of conditional suspension of the execution of an imprisonment sentence does not concern the execution of other sentences imposed in addition to this sentence. Probationary Period, Reasonable Restrictions and Reasonable Obligations
(1) In conditional sentencing, the court shall determine a probationary period of one year to five years, but not for a period shorter than the length of the conditionally suspended imprisonment sentence; the probationary period commences upon the final effect of the judgment. (2) In conditional sentencing, the court may impose on the offender the obligation to remain, during a specified part of the probationary period, following the commencement of the probationary period, in a specified dwelling or part thereof during a specified time period. The total duration of the conditionally sentenced person's stay in the specified dwelling or part thereof must not exceed one year, even in the case of determination of a longer probationary period. (3) The court may impose on the conditionally sentenced person reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also impose on him the obligation to make good, according to his means, the damage or to redress the non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender the unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (4) In the case of an offender of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures referred to in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions prescribed for juveniles. (5) The period during which the conditionally sentenced person led a proper life during the probationary period and complied with the imposed conditions shall be included in the probationary period newly determined in conditional sentencing for the same act or in the probationary period determined when imposing an aggregate or cumulative sentence or a joint sentence for continuation in a criminal offence; if the court imposes again the obligation to remain in a specified dwelling or part thereof during a specified time period, the period during which the convicted person properly performed this obligation shall be included in the duration of this obligation. Revocation of Reasonable Restrictions, Reasonable Obligations and Educational Measures
If a conditionally sentenced person leads a proper life and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed reasonable restriction, reasonable obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them; the court may make this decision at the earliest after one third of the probationary period, but at least after six months of its duration. The determined probationary period remains unaffected by the decision pursuant to the first sentence. Decision on Conditional Sentencing
(1) If a conditionally sentenced person has led a proper life during the probationary period and has complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the sentence shall be executed. Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, leave the conditional sentencing in force even though the convicted person has given cause for ordering the execution of the sentence, and
a) impose supervision over the convicted person,
b) reasonably extend the probationary period, but not by more than two years, provided that it does not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period prescribed in Section 82(1),
c) impose reasonable restrictions and reasonable obligations referred to in Section 48(4) not yet imposed.4 aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life,
d) impose educational measures not yet imposed pursuant to Section 82(4), if he is of an age close to the age of juveniles, or
e) order that the conditionally convicted person remain in a specified dwelling or part thereof pursuant to Section 82(2). (2) The provisions of Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the exercise of supervision. (3) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 1 within one year of the expiry of the probationary period, without the conditionally convicted person being at fault, the conditionally convicted person shall be deemed to have proved himself. (4) If it has been declared that the conditionally convicted person has proved himself, or if he is deemed to have proved himself, the offender shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. (5) If the court decides pursuant to paragraph 1 that the sentence is to be executed, it shall at the same time decide on the manner of execution of the sentence. Conditional suspension of execution of a sentence of imprisonment with supervision
If it is necessary to monitor and control the offender's conduct to an increased extent and to provide him with the necessary care and assistance during the probationary period, the court may, under the conditions set out in Section 81(1), conditionally suspend the execution of a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding three years while at the same time declaring supervision over the offender. The provisions of Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the exercise of supervision. Probationary period, appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations
(1) In the case of conditional conviction with supervision, the court shall set a probationary period of one to five years, but not for a period shorter than the length of the conditionally suspended sentence of imprisonment; the probationary period shall commence upon the entry into force of the judgment. (2) In the case of conditional conviction with supervision, the court may order the offender to remain, during a specified part of the probationary period following the commencement of the probationary period, in a specified dwelling or part thereof during a specified time period. The total duration of the conditionally convicted person's stay in a specified dwelling or part thereof may not exceed one year, even in the case of a longer probationary period being set. (3) The court may impose on a conditionally convicted person over whom supervision has been established appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; as a rule, it shall also order him to make good, according to his means, the damage or to remedy the non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. (4) If the offender is of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the appropriate restrictions or appropriate obligations referred to in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures set out in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions laid down for juveniles. Revocation of supervision or appropriate restrictions, appropriate obligations and educational measures
If a conditionally convicted person on whom supervision has been imposed leads a proper life during the probationary period and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed appropriate restriction, appropriate obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them, or may revoke the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and control his conduct to an increased extent; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the expiry of one third of the imposed probationary period, but at least after six months of its duration. The set probationary period shall remain unaffected by the decision pursuant to the first sentence. Decision on conditional conviction with supervision
(1) If a conditionally convicted person in respect of whom supervision has been declared has led a proper life during the probationary period and has complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the sentence is to be executed. Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, leave the conditional conviction with supervision in force even though the convicted person has given cause for ordering the execution of the sentence, and
a) establish further obligations over the convicted person within the framework of the imposed supervision,
b) extend the probationary period appropriately, but not by more than two years, and may not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period set out in Section 85(1),
c) impose appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations not yet imposed as referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life,
d) impose educational measures not yet imposed pursuant to Section 85(4), if he is of an age close to the age of juveniles, or
e) order that the conditionally convicted person remain in a specified dwelling or part thereof pursuant to Section 85(2). (2) If the court has not made a decision pursuant to paragraph 1 within one year of the expiry of the probationary period, without the conditionally convicted person being at fault, the conditionally convicted person shall be deemed to have proved himself. (3) If it has been declared that the conditionally convicted person has proved himself, or if he is deemed to have proved himself, the offender shall be regarded as if he had not been convicted. (4) If the court decides pursuant to paragraph 1 that the sentence is to be executed, it shall at the same time decide on the manner of execution of the sentence. Application mutatis mutandis of provisions on conditional conviction
The provisions on conditional conviction shall apply mutatis mutandis to the relationship of the conditional suspension of execution of a sentence of imprisonment to other sentences imposed in addition to this sentence pursuant to Section 81(2) and to the crediting of the period during which the conditionally convicted person with supervision led a proper life during the probationary period or properly performed the obligation to remain in a specified dwelling or part thereof during a specified time period, towards a new probationary period or towards the duration of this obligation if it is imposed again, pursuant to Section 82(5). Conditional release from execution of a sentence of imprisonment and conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events
Conditional release from execution of a sentence of imprisonment
(1) The court may conditionally release a convicted person if the convicted person, after the entry into force of the judgment, has demonstrated improvement, particularly during the execution of the sentence, by his conduct and fulfilment of his obligations, and it can be expected that he will lead a proper life in the future, or if the court accepts a guarantee for the completion of the convicted person's reform, and
a) the convicted person has served at least half of the imposed sentence of imprisonment or of the sentence mitigated by a decision of the President of the Czech Republic, or
b) the convicted person, who has not been convicted of a particularly serious felony,
1. by which death was or was to be caused, unless it is the criminal offence of manslaughter pursuant to Section 141(1),
2. by which serious bodily harm was or was to be caused,
3. the element of which is commission against a pregnant woman or a child,
4. which he committed as a member of an organised group, in association with an organised group or for the benefit of an organised criminal group, or the particularly serious felony of participation in an organised criminal group (Section 361),
5. which he committed with the intention of enabling or facilitating the commission of a terrorist criminal offence, the criminal offence of participation in a terrorist group (Section 312a), financing of terrorism (Section 312d), support and promotion of terrorism pursuant to Section 312e(3) or threatening a terrorist criminal offence (Section 312f), or
6. which is set out in Chapters Three, Seven, Nine, Twelve and Thirteen of the Special Part of this Act or in paragraph 4,
and who has not yet been in execution of a sentence of imprisonment, has served at least one third of the imposed sentence of imprisonment or of the sentence mitigated by a decision of the President of the Czech Republic. (2) If a convicted person for a misdemeanour has demonstrated by his exemplary conduct and fulfilment of his obligations that further execution of the sentence is not necessary, the court may conditionally release him even before he has served the part of the sentence of imprisonment required for conditional release pursuant to paragraph 1. The court shall not grant a proposal by the prison director for conditional release of a convicted person if it is obvious that the convicted person would not lead a proper life after release. (3) When deciding on the conditional release of a convicted person for a felony, the court shall also take into account whether the convicted person commenced execution of the sentence in time and whether he has partially or fully made good or otherwise remedied the damage or other harm caused by the criminal offence, or whether he has surrendered unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. If the convicted person underwent protective treatment before commencing execution of the sentence of imprisonment or during its course, the court shall also take into account the convicted person's demonstrated attitude towards the execution of protective treatment. (4) A person convicted of the criminal offence of murder (Section 140), manslaughter pursuant to Section 141(2), grievous bodily harm pursuant to Section 145(3), torture and other inhuman and cruel treatment pursuant to Section 149(4), unlawful termination of pregnancy without the consent of the pregnant woman pursuant to Section 159(3), (4), unauthorised removal of tissues and organs pursuant to Section 164(3), (4), trafficking in human beings pursuant to Section 168(4), (5), deprivation of personal liberty pursuant to Section 170(2), (3), abduction pursuant to Section 172(2), (3), robbery pursuant to Section 173(3), (4), hostage taking pursuant to Section 174(3), (4), extortion pursuant to Section 175(3), (4), rape pursuant to Section 185(3), (4), sexual abuse pursuant to Section 187(3), (4), general endangerment pursuant to Section 272(2), (3), unauthorised production and other handling of narcotic and psychotropic substances and poisons pursuant to Section 283(4), seizure of control of an aircraft, civilian vessel and fixed platform (Section 290), abduction of an aircraft abroad pursuant to Section 292(3), high treason (Section 309), subversion of the republic (Section 310), terrorist attack (Section 311), terror (Section 312), sabotage (Section 314), espionage pursuant to Section 316(3), (4), war treason (Section 320), forcible crossing of the state border pursuant to Section 339(3), organising and enabling unlawful crossing of the state border pursuant to Section 340(4), genocide (Section 400), attack against humanity (Section 401), apartheid and discrimination of a group of people (Section 402), aggression (Section 405a), preparation of an aggressive war (Section 406), contacts endangering peace (Section 409), use of a prohibited means of combat and unlawful conduct of combat pursuant to Section 411(3), war cruelty (Section 412), persecution of the population (Section 413), plundering in the area of war operations (Section 414) or abuse of internationally recognised and state symbols pursuant to Section 415(3), as well as a person sentenced to an exceptional sentence of imprisonment of more than twenty up to thirty years (Section 54(2)), may be conditionally released only after serving two thirds of the imposed sentence of imprisonment, unless, having regard to the circumstances of the act for which they were convicted and the nature of their personality, there is a threat of repetition of the committed or other similar particularly serious felony. (5) A person sentenced to an exceptional sentence of imprisonment for life may be conditionally released only after at least twenty years of execution of this sentence, unless, having regard to the circumstances of the act for which they were convicted and the nature of their personality, there is a threat of repetition of the committed or other similar particularly serious felony. Probationary period and appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations upon conditional release
(1) Upon conditional release, the court shall set a probationary period of up to three years for persons convicted of a misdemeanour and of one to seven years for persons convicted of a felony; the probationary period shall commence with the conditional release of the convicted person. The court may at the same time declare supervision over the offender and simultaneously order that, during a specified part of the probationary period following the commencement of the probationary period, the offender remain in a specified dwelling or part thereof during a specified time period. Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the exercise of supervision. (2) The court may impose on a conditionally released person appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life; it may also order him to make good, according to his means, the damage or to remedy the non-pecuniary harm caused by the criminal offence, or to surrender unjust enrichment obtained by the criminal offence. If the conditionally released person is of an age close to the age of juveniles, the court may, in the interest of utilising the educational influence of the family, school and other entities, impose, either independently or in addition to the appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations referred to in Section 48(4), also some of the educational measures set out in the Act on Juvenile Justice, applying mutatis mutandis the conditions laid down for juveniles. The court may order a person conditionally released pursuant to Section 88(2) to remain, during a specified part of the probationary period following the commencement of the probationary period, in a specified dwelling or part thereof during a specified time period, or to perform work for the benefit of providers of community service work, or to pay a specified sum of money to assist victims of criminal activity into the court's account. (3) The total duration of the specified stay of the conditionally released person in a specified dwelling or part thereof pursuant to paragraphs 1 and 2 may not exceed one year, even in the case of a longer probationary period being set. The performance of work pursuant to paragraph 2 may be set at between 50 and 200 hours. The sum for financial assistance to victims of criminal activity pursuant to paragraph 2 shall be set at between CZK 2,000 and CZK 10,000,000; when setting this sum, the court shall also take into account the personal and property circumstances of the convicted person and, having regard thereto, may determine that the specified sum will be paid in appropriate monthly instalments. (4) If a conditionally released person leads a proper life during the probationary period and has demonstrated improvement by fulfilling his obligations, the court may revoke the imposed appropriate restriction, appropriate obligation or educational measure if it can be expected that he will lead a proper life even without them, or may revoke the imposed supervision if it is no longer necessary to monitor and control his conduct to an increased extent; the court may make this decision at the earliest after the expiry of one third of the imposed probationary period, but at least after six months of its duration. The set probationary period shall remain unaffected by the decision pursuant to the first sentence. Conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events
(1) After execution of half of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events, the court may conditionally waive execution of the remainder thereof if the convicted person has demonstrated by his way of life during the execution of the sentence that further execution of this sentence is not necessary, or if the court accepts a guarantee for the completion of the convicted person's reform. When deciding on conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, the court may take into account whether the convicted person has successfully undergone an appropriate programme of social training and re-education or psychological counselling focused on the activity which is the subject of the prohibition. (2) Upon conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events, the court shall set a probationary period of up to five years, but not for a period shorter than the remainder of the sentence; the probationary period shall commence upon the entry into force of the decision on this waiver. Common provision
(1) If a conditionally released person or a convicted person in respect of whom execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events has been conditionally waived has led a proper life during the probationary period and has complied with the imposed conditions, the court shall declare that he has proved himself; otherwise it shall decide, possibly already during the probationary period, that the remainder of the sentence is to be executed. Exceptionally, the court may, having regard to the circumstances of the case and the person of the convicted person, leave the conditional release or conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events in force even though the convicted person has given cause for a decision that the remainder of the sentence is to be executed, and
a) establish supervision over the convicted person,
b) extend the probationary period appropriately, but not by more than two years, and may not exceed the upper limit of the probationary period set out in Section 89(1) or Section 90(2), or
c) impose appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations not yet imposed as referred to in Section 48(4) aimed at ensuring that he leads a proper life. (2) Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the exercise of supervision. (3) If the court has declared that a conditionally released person or a convicted person in respect of whom execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events has been conditionally waived has proved himself, the sentence shall be deemed to have been executed on the day on which he was conditionally released or on which the decision to conditionally waive execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events entered into force. (4) In the case of a conditionally released person or a convicted person in respect of whom execution of the remainder of a sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events has been conditionally waived, the sentence shall also be deemed to have been executed on the day on which the conditional release occurred or on which the decision to conditionally waive execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events entered into force, if the court has not, through no fault of his, made a decision pursuant to paragraph 1 within one year of the expiry of the probationary period. (5) Repeated conditional release from execution of the same sentence is possible after execution of half of the remainder of the sentence, and in the cases referred to in Section 88(4) after execution of two thirds of the remainder of the sentence. Repeated conditional release from an exceptional sentence of imprisonment for life is not possible. (6) If the guarantee for the completion of the convicted person's reform has been revoked by the person who provided it, the court shall review the convicted person's conduct during the probationary period, and if it finds that the conditional release or conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events is not fulfilling its purpose, it shall decide that the remainder of the sentence is to be executed; otherwise it shall leave the conditional release or conditional waiver of execution of the remainder of the sentence of prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence or prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events in force. Crediting of custody and sentence
Crediting of custody and sentence
(1) If criminal proceedings were conducted against the offender in custody and he is convicted in these proceedings, the period spent in custody shall be credited towards the imposed sentence, or towards an aggregate or cumulative sentence, if crediting is possible having regard to the type of sentence imposed. (2) If the offender was punished by a court or other authority and a new conviction occurs for the same act, the sentence served shall be credited towards the imposed sentence, if crediting is possible having regard to the type of sentence imposed. The court shall proceed similarly if it has imposed on the offender an aggregate or cumulative sentence (Section 43) or a joint sentence for continuation of a criminal offence (Section 45). (3) If crediting of custody or sentence is not possible, the court shall take this fact into account when determining the type of sentence or its extent. (4) The provisions of paragraphs 1 to 3 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the crediting of preliminary, extradition and surrender custody executed pursuant to the Act on International Judicial Cooperation in Criminal Matters and to the crediting of the obligation to remain in a specified dwelling or part thereof during a specified time period imposed on the accused pursuant to Section 73(3) of the Code of Criminal Procedure towards the sentence imposed for the same act. Crediting of custody and sentence executed abroad
(1) If, for the same act, the offender was in custody abroad or was punished by an authority of a foreign state or an international judicial body, the period spent in custody or the sentence served shall be credited towards the sentence imposed by a court of the Czech Republic, if crediting is possible having regard to the type of sentence imposed. The court shall proceed similarly if it has imposed on the offender an aggregate or cumulative sentence (Section 43) or a joint sentence for continuation of a criminal offence (Section 45). (2) If crediting of custody or a sentence imposed abroad (paragraph 1) is not possible, in particular because a sentence of such a type as the Criminal Code does not recognise was executed in whole or in part abroad, the court shall take this fact into account when determining the type of sentence or its extent. Extinction of execution of sentence
Limitation of execution of sentence
(1) An imposed sentence cannot be executed after the expiry of the limitation period, which is
a) thirty years in the case of conviction to an exceptional sentence,
b) twenty years in the case of conviction to a sentence of imprisonment exceeding ten years,
c) ten years in the case of conviction to a sentence of imprisonment of at least five years,
d) five years in the case of conviction to another sentence. (2) The limitation period shall commence upon the entry into force of the judgment, and in the case of conditional conviction or conditional release, upon the entry into force of the decision that the sentence is to be executed. (3) The limitation period shall not include the period during which it was not possible to execute the sentence because the convicted person was staying abroad, was undergoing institutional protective treatment or security detention, or another sentence of imprisonment was being executed on him. As regards a pecuniary sentence, prohibition of activity, prohibition of keeping and breeding animals, prohibition of residence, prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events and expulsion, the limitation period shall also not include the period during which a sentence of imprisonment was being executed on the convicted person, and in the case of a sentence of expulsion, the period during which execution of this sentence was suspended. (4) The limitation period shall be interrupted,
a) if the court takes measures aimed at the execution of the sentence in respect of which limitation is concerned, or
b) if the convicted person commits a new criminal offence during the limitation period for which the Criminal Code provides a sentence of the same or more severe nature. (5) Upon interruption of the limitation period, a new limitation period shall commence. Exclusion from limitation
Execution of a sentence imposed for criminal offences referred to in Section 35 is not subject to limitation. Protective measures
General principles for imposing protective measures
Principle of proportionality
(1) A protective measure may not be imposed if it is not proportionate to the nature and seriousness of the act committed by the offender and the danger which the offender poses in the future to interests protected by the Criminal Code, as well as to the person of the offender and his circumstances. (2) The harm caused by an imposed and executed protective measure must not be greater than is necessary to achieve its purpose. Imposition of protective measures
(1) Protective measures may be imposed, upon fulfilment of the statutory conditions, independently or in addition to a sentence. (2) In addition to a sentence of a similar nature, a protective measure may be imposed only if its independent imposition would not be sufficient from the point of view of influencing the person on whom it is imposed and the protection of society. (3) If the conditions for imposing several protective measures are met, they may be imposed alongside each other, unless the Criminal Code provides otherwise. However, if the necessary influence on the person on whom it is imposed and adequate protection of society can be achieved by only one of them, only such a protective measure shall be imposed. (4) If several protective measures are imposed alongside each other which cannot be executed simultaneously, the court shall determine the order of their execution. Protective measures and their imposition
Types of protective measures
(1) Protective measures are protective treatment, security detention, confiscation of a thing, confiscation of part of property and protective upbringing. (2) The imposition of protective upbringing is regulated by the Act on Juvenile Justice. (3) Protective treatment may not be imposed in addition to security detention. Confiscation of part of property may not be imposed in addition to forfeiture of the same part of property. Protective treatment
(1) The court shall impose protective treatment in the case referred to in Section 40(2) and Section 47(1), or if the offender of an otherwise criminal act is not criminally responsible due to insanity and his presence at liberty is dangerous. (2) The court may also impose protective treatment if
a) the offender committed the criminal offence in a state induced by a mental disorder and his presence at liberty is dangerous, or
b) the offender who abuses an addictive substance committed the criminal offence under its influence or in connection with its abuse; however, it shall not impose protective treatment if, having regard to the person of the offender, it is clear that its purpose cannot be achieved. (3) The court may impose protective treatment also in addition to a sentence or upon waiver of punishment. (4) According to the nature of the illness and treatment possibilities, the court shall impose institutional or outpatient protective treatment. If, in addition to institutional protective treatment, a sentence of imprisonment has been imposed, the protective treatment shall as a rule be executed after commencement of execution of the sentence of imprisonment in a prison. If protective treatment cannot be executed after commencement of execution of the sentence in a prison, institutional protective treatment shall be executed in a healthcare facility before commencement of execution of the sentence of imprisonment if this better ensures fulfilment of the purpose of treatment, otherwise it shall be executed in a healthcare facility after execution or other termination of the sentence of imprisonment. Outpatient protective treatment shall as a rule be executed after commencement of execution of the sentence of imprisonment in a prison; if execution of outpatient protective treatment in a prison cannot be carried out, it shall be executed only after execution of the sentence of imprisonment. If the length of execution of the sentence of imprisonment in a prison is not sufficient to fulfil the purpose of protective treatment, the court may decide on its continuation in a healthcare facility providing institutional or outpatient care. (5) The court may subsequently change institutional treatment to outpatient treatment and vice versa. The court may change institutional protective treatment to security detention under the conditions of Section 100(1) or (2). Without the conditions of Section 100(1) or (2), the court may change institutional protective treatment to security detention if the imposed and executed protective treatment is not fulfilling its purpose or does not ensure sufficient protection of society, in particular in the case that the offender escaped from the healthcare facility, used violence against employees of the healthcare facility or other persons during execution of protective treatment, or repeatedly refused diagnostic or therapeutic procedures. (6) Protective treatment shall last as long as its purpose requires. Institutional protective treatment shall last at most two years; if treatment is not completed within this period, the court shall decide before the end of this period on its extension, also repeatedly, always for at most a further two years; otherwise it shall decide on release from protective treatment or on changing institutional treatment to outpatient treatment, unless the offender is at fault for the court not being able to decide within the stated period; in such a case, the court shall decide without delay after the obstacle preventing the decision has ceased. The duration of protective treatment imposed pursuant to paragraph 2(b) may be terminated as soon as it is established during its execution that its purpose cannot be achieved; if there is a danger that the convicted person will commit another criminal offence, the court shall, in the decision on release from protective treatment, impose on the convicted person supervision of his conduct for up to five years; the provisions of Sections 49 to 51 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the exercise of supervision. The court shall decide on release from protective treatment. (7) The court shall waive execution of protective treatment if the circumstances for which it was imposed cease before its commencement. Security detention
(1) The court shall impose security detention in the case referred to in Section 47(2), or if the offender of an otherwise criminal act which would fulfil the elements of a felony is not criminally responsible due to insanity, his presence at liberty is dangerous, and it cannot be expected that the imposed protective treatment, having regard to the nature of the mental disorder and the possibilities of influencing the offender, would lead to sufficient protection of society. (2) The court may impose security detention, having regard to the person of the offender and taking into account his previous life and his circumstances, also if
a) the offender committed a felony in a state induced by a mental disorder, his presence at liberty is dangerous, and it cannot be expected that the imposed protective treatment, having regard to the nature of the mental disorder and the possibilities of influencing the offender, would lead to sufficient protection of society, or
b) the offender who indulges in abuse of an addictive substance has again committed a felony, even though he has already been convicted of a felony committed under the influence of an addictive substance or in connection with its abuse to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment of at least two years, and it cannot be expected that sufficient protection of society could be achieved by imposing protective treatment, also having regard to the offender's already demonstrated attitude towards protective treatment. (3) The court may impose security detention independently, upon waiver of punishment, or also in addition to a sentence. If security detention has been imposed in addition to an unconditional sentence of imprisonment, it shall be executed after execution or other termination of the sentence of imprisonment. If an unconditional sentence of imprisonment was imposed during execution of security detention, its execution shall be suspended for the duration of execution of such sentence of imprisonment. After completion of execution of the sentence, execution of security detention shall continue. (4) Security detention shall be executed in an institution for execution of security detention with special security and with therapeutic, psychological, educational, pedagogical, rehabilitation and activity programmes. (5) Security detention shall last as long as the protection of society requires. The court shall review at least once every twelve months, and in the case of juveniles once every six months, whether the reasons for its further continuation still persist. (6) The court may subsequently change security detention to institutional protective treatment if the reasons for which it was imposed cease and the conditions for institutional protective treatment are simultaneously met. (7) The court shall waive execution of security detention if the circumstances for which it was imposed cease before its commencement. Confiscation of a thing
(1) If a sentence of forfeiture of a thing referred to in Section 70(2)(a) has not been imposed, the court may order that such a thing be confiscated,
a) if it belongs to the offender who cannot be prosecuted or convicted,
b) if it belongs to the offender from whose punishment the court has waived, or
c) if it endangers the safety of people or property, or society, or there is a danger that it will be used to commit a felony. (2) The court may order confiscation of a thing which is a direct product of criminal activity or is an indirect product of criminal activity, provided that the value of the thing constituting the direct product of criminal activity is not negligible in relation to the value of the thing constituting the indirect product of criminal activity, and provided that such a thing
a) belongs to the offender who has been convicted of the criminal offence from which the thing originates,
b) belongs to the offender who cannot be prosecuted or convicted,
c) belongs to the offender from whose punishment the court has waived,
d) belongs to an insane person who committed an otherwise criminal act,
e) belongs to another person to whom the offender transferred such a thing or who otherwise acquired it, or
f) forms part of property in a trust fund or similar arrangement (hereinafter "trust fund") or in a mutual fund. (3) If the offender or another person holds, in breach of another legal regulation, a thing referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 in respect of which confiscation of a thing may be imposed, the court shall always impose this protective measure on him. (4) The court may, instead of confiscation of a thing, impose an obligation to
a) modify the thing so that it cannot be used for a socially dangerous purpose,
b) destroy it,
c) remove its marking or carry out its alteration, or
d) hand it over to a designated authority,
and shall set an appropriate time limit for this. (5) If the obligation set pursuant to paragraph 4 is not fulfilled within the set time limit, the court shall decide on confiscation of the thing. Confiscation of substitute value
If the person from whom a thing could be confiscated pursuant to Section 101(1) or (2) destroys, damages or otherwise devalues it, alienates it, renders it unusable, removes it or utilises it, in particular consumes it, or otherwise frustrates its confiscation before a decision on confiscation, or if he frustrates a sentence of forfeiture of a thing by conduct breaching the prohibition pursuant to Section 70(5), or frustrates confiscation of a thing by conduct breaching the prohibition pursuant to Section 104(2), the court may impose on him confiscation of substitute value up to an amount corresponding to the value of such a thing. The court may determine the value of a thing whose confiscation it could have imposed on the basis of an expert opinion or expert report. Confiscation of part of property
(1) The court may impose confiscation of part of property on an offender who has been found guilty of an intentional criminal offence for which the Criminal Code provides a sentence of imprisonment with an upper limit of the sentencing range of at least four years, or of the criminal offence of production and other handling of child pornography (Section 192), unauthorised access to a computer system and unauthorised interference with a computer system or information carrier (Section 230), procurement and possession of access equipment and password to a computer system and other such data (Section 231), machinations in the award of a public contract and in a public competition (Section 257), machinations in a public auction (Section 258), unauthorised cultivation of plants containing a narcotic or psychotropic substance pursuant to Section 285(2 to 4, spreading drug addiction (§ 287), bribery (§ 332) or indirect bribery (§ 333), if the offender by such a criminal offence obtained or attempted to obtain a pecuniary benefit for himself or for another and the court is of the opinion that a certain part of his property originates from criminal activity in view of the fact that the value of the property which the offender acquired or transferred to another person or into property in a trust fund within a period of no more than 5 years before the commission of such a criminal offence, during its commission or after its commission, is grossly disproportionate to the offender's income acquired in accordance with the law or other facts justifying such a conclusion have been established. (2) The court may impose confiscation of part of the property in relation to a thing which could otherwise be confiscated under paragraph 1, if the offender transferred such thing
a) to another person gratuitously or under conspicuously advantageous conditions and that person knew or could and should have known that he transferred such thing to him for the reason of avoiding its confiscation, or that such thing was acquired in violation of the law,
c) to a legal person in which he himself or in conjunction with persons close to him has a majority proprietary interest, a majority share of voting rights or decisive influence on management, and the offender uses such thing gratuitously or under conspicuously advantageous conditions,
d) into property in a trust fund, or
e) acquired it into the community property of spouses. (3) The court, when determining the part of the property whose confiscation it imposes, shall specify the particular things subject to confiscation. If a gross disproportion has been established between the value of the property and the offender's income acquired in accordance with the law in the monitored period, it may thus specify any things of the offender up to the amount of the established gross disproportion. (4) If it is not known where the things which could be confiscated are located, or their confiscation is not appropriate especially with regard to the rights of third parties, or if the person from whom the thing could be confiscated destroys, damages, alienates it or otherwise frustrates its confiscation before the decision on confiscation, the court may impose confiscation of substitute value, including a monetary amount, up to the amount corresponding to the value of such thing. The value of the thing whose confiscation the court could have imposed shall be determined by the court on the basis of an expert statement or expert opinion. (5) Confiscation also affects the fruits and benefits of the thing which belong to the person from whom such thing is being confiscated. Confiscation does not extend to things which are necessary to satisfy the living needs of the person from whom part of the property is being confiscated, or of persons for whose maintenance or upbringing this person is obliged to care according to law. Confiscation of writings and devices
(1) A writing or writings with such content whose intentional dissemination with knowledge of their content would fulfil the elements of a criminal offence under the criminal law shall be confiscated pursuant to § 101 paragraph 1, if at least one copy of the writing has already been disseminated, intended for dissemination or prepared. At the same time, a device used or intended for the production of such writings shall also be confiscated, in particular a printer, plate, form, typesetting, block, negative, matrix, computer program or copying device; the provisions of § 101 paragraphs 4 and 5 shall apply mutatis mutandis. (2) Confiscation extends only to the copy or copies of the writing which are in the possession of persons cooperating in their dissemination or in their preparation for dissemination, or which have been published by display, posting, presentation or other similar manner, or which have not yet been delivered to the recipient in mail-order dissemination. (3) Confiscation of a writing or writings under paragraphs 1 and 2 shall apply mutatis mutandis also in the case where their intentional dissemination with knowledge of their content could constitute a criminal offence if further circumstances occur which, however, were not fulfilled in the case under consideration, and confiscation of the writing or writings is at the same time necessary to prevent their unlawful dissemination. Effect of confiscation
(1) A confiscated thing, confiscated part of property, confiscated substitute value, confiscated writing or device shall pass to the state; liens on them shall not cease. (2) The provision of § 70 paragraph 5 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the imposition of confiscation of a thing, confiscation of part of property and to the imposition of an obligation under § 101 paragraph 4; the prohibition of alienation here applies until the fulfilment of the obligation under § 101 paragraph 4, and if this obligation has not been fulfilled, until the legal force of the decision on confiscation of the thing (§ 101 paragraph 5). EXPUNGEMENT OF CONVICTION
Conditions for expungement of conviction
(1) The court shall expunge a conviction if the convicted person has led a proper life continuously after the execution or remission of the sentence or after the limitation of its execution for a period of at least
a) fifteen years, in the case of a conviction for an exceptional sentence,
b) ten years, in the case of a conviction for a sentence of imprisonment exceeding five years,
c) five years, in the case of a conviction for a sentence of imprisonment exceeding one year,
d) three years, in the case of a conviction for a sentence of imprisonment not exceeding one year or for a sentence of expulsion,
e) one year, in the case of a conviction for a sentence of house arrest, for a sentence of forfeiture of property, for a sentence of forfeiture of a thing, for a sentence of prohibition of residence, for a sentence of prohibition of entry to sporting, cultural and other social events or for a monetary sentence for a particularly serious felony. (2) In the case of a conviction for loss of honorary titles or decorations or for loss of military rank, the period referred to in paragraph 1 shall be governed by the length of the sentence of imprisonment in addition to which the said sentence was imposed. (3) If the convicted person has demonstrated by his very good conduct after the execution or remission of the sentence or the limitation of its execution that he has reformed, the court may, having regard to the interests protected by the criminal law, expunge the conviction at the request of the convicted person or of a person entitled to offer a guarantee for the completion of the convicted person's reform, even before the expiry of the period referred to in paragraph 1. (4) The period referred to in paragraph 1 shall, in the case where a conditionally released person is deemed to have served the sentence on the day on which the convicted person was conditionally released, be governed by the length of the actual execution of the sentence; in the case where the sentence was reduced by a decision of the President of the Czech Republic, it shall be governed by the length of the sentence thus reduced. (5) If several sentences have been imposed on the offender concurrently, the conviction cannot be expunged unless the period for expungement of the sentence for which this Act provides the longest period has elapsed. (6) The provision of paragraph 5 shall apply mutatis mutandis also to the case where several sentences have been imposed on the offender concurrently for which, according to this Act, the effect may occur that the offender is regarded as not having been convicted. (7) Even if the period referred to in paragraph 1 has elapsed, the conviction cannot be expunged if the imposed protective measure has not yet been executed or otherwise terminated; this provision shall apply mutatis mutandis also where the criminal law provides that the offender is regarded as not having been convicted. Effects of expungement
If a conviction has been expunged, the offender shall be regarded as not having been convicted. SPECIAL PROVISIONS ON CERTAIN OFFENDERS
Offender of a criminal offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group
(1) An offender of a criminal offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group (§ 129) is a person who committed an intentional criminal offence as a member of an organised criminal group, or a person who committed such an act knowingly with a member of an organised criminal group or with the intention of assisting an organised criminal group. (2) The fact that the offender committed the criminal offence as a member of an organised group or in conjunction with an organised group does not prevent him from being punished, if the conditions laid down by this Act are met, as an offender of a criminal offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group at the same time. Imposition of a sentence of imprisonment on an offender of a criminal offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group
(1) The upper limit of the sentencing range for a sentence of imprisonment provided for in the criminal law shall be increased by one third for an offender of a criminal offence committed in favour of an organised criminal group. The court shall impose on such an offender a sentence of imprisonment in the upper half of the sentencing range for imprisonment thus established, unless the conditions for an extraordinary reduction of the sentence of imprisonment under § 58 are also met. (2) The upper limit of the sentencing range for a sentence of imprisonment may, after the increase under paragraph 1, exceed twenty years. When imposing an exceptional sentence of imprisonment above twenty years up to thirty years, the upper limit must not exceed thirty years. Juveniles
The criminal liability of juveniles and the sanctions imposed on them are regulated by the Act on Juvenile Justice. Unless the Act on Juvenile Justice provides otherwise, this Act shall apply. INTERPRETATIVE PROVISIONS
Criminal law
Criminal law means this Act and, according to the nature of the matter, also the Act on Juvenile Justice and the Act on Criminal Liability of Legal Persons and Proceedings Against Them. On the concept of criminal offence
A criminal offence means only a judicially punishable act, and unless otherwise provided by an individual provision of the criminal law, also preparation for a criminal offence, attempt of a criminal offence, organising, instigation and aiding. Omission
An act means also an omission of such conduct to which the offender was obliged under another legal regulation, official decision or contract, as a result of voluntary assumption of the obligation to act or if such his special obligation arose from his previous endangering conduct or to which he was obliged for another reason according to the circumstances and his situation. On the concept of offender
An offender means, unless otherwise provided by an individual provision of the criminal law, also a co-offender and a participant. Specific and special subject
(1) If the criminal law requires a special quality, capacity or position of the offender for the commission of a criminal offence, only a person who has the required quality, capacity or position can be an offender or co-offender of the criminal offence. Only a soldier can be an offender or co-offender of a military criminal offence under Chapter Twelve of the Special Part of this Act. (2) If the law provides that the offender must be a bearer of a special quality, capacity or position, it is sufficient that the special quality, capacity or position are present in the legal person on whose behalf the offender acts. This provision shall also apply if
a) the offender's act occurred before the establishment of the legal person,
b) the legal person was established but the court decided on the invalidity of the legal person, or
c) the legal act which was to establish the authorisation to act for the legal person is invalid or ineffective. (3) An organiser, instigator or aider of a criminal offence referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2 may also be a person who does not have the quality, capacity or position required therein. (4) Where this Act refers to a soldier, it means
b) a soldier outside active service, if he is in service uniform,
c) a member of a security corps in the case of the criminal offences of disobedience to an order (§ 375), disobedience to an order by negligence (§ 376), insult between soldiers (§ 378), insult between soldiers by violence or threat of violence (§ 379), insult of a soldier of equal rank by violence or threat of violence (§ 380), violence against a superior (§ 381), breach of duty of guard service (§ 389), breach of duty of supervisory or other service (§ 390) and establishment, support and promotion of a movement aimed at suppressing human rights and freedoms under § 403 paragraph 2 letter c), or
(5) Where this Act refers to military service or military duty, it means the service or duty of the persons referred to in paragraph 4. Unauthorised absence
a) does not report for duty at the appointed time,
b) absents himself from duty without permission,
c) is separated in a combat situation from the place of duty and after the cessation of the cause of separation does not report to any superior or other military unit, even in the case of return from captivity or after release from captivity. Continuing criminal offence
A continuing criminal offence means such conduct whose individual partial attacks guided by a single intention fulfil, even in aggregate, the elements of the same criminal offence, are connected by the same or similar manner of execution and by close temporal connection and connection in the object of the attack. Public commission of a criminal offence
A criminal offence is committed publicly if it is committed
a) by the content of a printed matter or disseminated writing, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective manner, or
b) in the presence of at least three persons simultaneously present. Commission of a criminal offence with a weapon
