A criminal offence is committed with a weapon if the offender or, with his knowledge, one of the co-offenders uses a weapon for attack, to overcome or prevent resistance or if he has it with him for that purpose; a weapon here means, unless otherwise provided by an individual provision of the criminal law, anything by which an attack on the body can be made more forceful. Commission of a criminal offence by violence
A criminal offence is committed by violence also if it is committed on a person whom the offender has rendered defenceless by deceit or other similar manner. Misleading someone and exploiting someone's error by means of a technical device
Misleading someone or exploiting someone's error can also be done by interfering with data stored in a computer system or on an information carrier, interfering with the software or technical equipment of a computer system or performing another operation in a computer system, interfering with electronic or other technical equipment, including interfering with objects used to control such equipment, or by exploiting such an operation or such interference performed by another. Breaking in
Breaking in means entering a closed space by deceit, unlawful overcoming of locking or overcoming another securing obstacle using force. Bodily harm and serious bodily harm
(1) Bodily harm means such a condition consisting in an impairment of health or other illness which, by disrupting normal bodily or mental functions, makes the usual way of life of the injured person more difficult, not only for a short time, and which requires medical treatment. (2) Serious bodily harm means only a serious impairment of health or other serious illness. Under these conditions, serious bodily harm is
b) loss or substantial reduction of working capacity,
d) loss or substantial weakening of the function of a sensory organ,
e) damage to an important organ,
g) causing a miscarriage or killing a foetus,
i) a long-lasting impairment of health. Mental disorder
Mental disorder means, in addition to mental disorders arising from mental illness, also profound disturbance of consciousness, mental retardation, severe antisocial personality disorder or other severe mental or sexual deviation. State-imposed and recognised duty of confidentiality
A state-imposed or recognised duty of confidentiality is considered to be confidentiality which is imposed or recognised by another legal regulation. A state-recognised duty of confidentiality is not considered under the criminal law to be such a duty whose scope is not defined by another legal regulation but arises from a legal act performed on the basis of another legal regulation. Person close
A person close means a relative in direct line, adopter, adoptee, sibling, spouse and partner; other persons in a family or similar relationship are considered to be persons close to each other only if the harm suffered by one of them would be reasonably felt by the other as his own harm. Child
A child means a person under eighteen years of age, unless the criminal law provides otherwise. Official person
c) the President of the Czech Republic, a deputy or senator of the Parliament of the Czech Republic, a member of the Government of the Czech Republic or another person holding a function in another public authority,
d) a member of a council or a responsible official of territorial self-government, a state administration body or another public authority,
e) a member of the armed forces or a security corps or a municipal police officer,
f) a judicial executor in the performance of execution activity and in activities performed on behalf of a court or public prosecutor,
g) a notary when performing acts in inheritance proceedings as a judicial commissioner,
h) a financial arbitrator and his deputy,
i) a natural person who has been appointed as a forest guard, nature guard, hunting guard or fishing guard,
if he performs tasks of the state or society and uses the authority entrusted to him for the performance of these tasks. (2) For criminal liability and protection of an official person, individual provisions of the criminal law require that the criminal offence was committed in connection with his authority and responsibility. (3) An official person of a foreign state or international organisation is considered an official person under the criminal law under the conditions referred to in paragraphs 1 and 2, if so provided by an international treaty or if he acts on its territory with the consent of the authorities of the Czech Republic; this consent is not required in the case of an official person of an international criminal court, international criminal tribunal or similar international judicial body which meets at least one of the conditions referred to in § 145 paragraph 1 letter a) of the Act on International Judicial Cooperation in Criminal Matters. Public prosecutor
A public prosecutor means also a European Delegated Prosecutor, a European Prosecutor and a European Chief Prosecutor within the scope of their competence established by a directly applicable regulation of the European Union regulating the establishment of the European Public Prosecutor's Office. Insolvency administrator and insolvency proceedings
(1) An insolvency administrator means also a preliminary insolvency administrator, deputy insolvency administrator, separate insolvency administrator, special insolvency administrator; the position of insolvency administrator is also held by a bankruptcy trustee, preliminary bankruptcy trustee, special bankruptcy trustee, deputy bankruptcy trustee and composition administrator. An insolvency administrator also means a person whom, according to another legal regulation, the insolvency administrator has designated to represent him in the exercise of his authority established by another legal regulation on the territory of another state, furthermore a foreign insolvency administrator, a foreign insolvency administrator of an insurance company or reinsurance company and a person whom, according to another legal regulation, the foreign insolvency administrator or foreign insolvency administrator of an insurance company or reinsurance company has appointed to assist or represent him. (2) Insolvency proceedings mean also bankruptcy and composition proceedings. Organised criminal group
An organised criminal group is an association of at least three criminally responsible persons with an internal organisational structure, with division of functions and division of activities, which is focused on the systematic commission of intentional criminal activity. Terrorist group
(1) A terrorist group is an association of at least three criminally responsible persons which has a more permanent character, in which there is a division of activities among its individual members, its activity is characterised by planning and coordination and is focused on the commission of the criminal offence of high treason committed in the form of a terrorist attack or terror (§ 309), the criminal offence of terrorist attack (§ 311) or the criminal offence of terror (§ 312) (hereinafter "terrorist criminal offence"). (2) The fulfilment of the elements of a terrorist group does not preclude the application of the provisions of this and other laws on an organised group and an organised criminal group; the provisions of § 361 and 363 shall not apply. Addictive substance
An addictive substance means alcohol, narcotic drugs, psychotropic substances and other substances capable of adversely affecting a person's psyche or his control or recognition abilities or social behaviour. Public document
(1) A public document means a document issued by a court of the Czech Republic, another public authority or another entity authorised or empowered to do so by another legal regulation within the limits of its authority, confirming that it is an order or declaration of the body or other entity which issued the document, or certifying a legally significant fact. A public document is also a document which another legal regulation declares to be public. (2) Protection under § 348 is also provided to a public document issued by a public authority or other entity authorised to do so of a foreign state or by a body of an international organisation, if it has effects on the territory of the Czech Republic under an international treaty. Public utility facility
A public utility facility means a public protective facility against fire, flood or other natural disaster, a defensive or protective facility against air and other similar attacks or their consequences, a protective facility against the escape of pollutants, an energy or water supply facility, a submarine cable or submarine pipeline, electronic communications equipment and networks and terminal telecommunications and radio equipment, equipment of a postal licence holder, equipment for public transport, including components of a railway and railway vehicles in public railway transport and vertical prohibition or command traffic signs and traffic signs regulating priority. Dwelling
A dwelling means a house, flat or other space used for housing and accessories belonging to them. Thing
A thing means also a controllable natural force. Provisions on things also apply to live animals and processed separated parts of the human body, unless otherwise provided by individual provisions of the criminal law. Thing belonging to the offender
A thing belongs to the offender if he owns it at the time of the decision on it, it is part of his property or he actually deals with it as an owner without the authorised owner or holder of such thing being known. Instrument of criminal activity
An instrument of criminal activity means a thing which was intended or used for the commission of a criminal offence, including fruits and benefits. Proceeds of criminal activity
(1) Proceeds of criminal activity means any economic advantage originating from a criminal offence. (2) Direct proceeds of criminal activity means a thing which was obtained by a criminal offence or as a reward for it, including fruits and benefits. (3) Indirect proceeds of criminal activity means a thing, including fruits and benefits,
a) which was, even if only in part, acquired for a thing constituting direct proceeds of criminal activity,
b) into which a thing constituting direct proceeds of criminal activity was, even if only in part, transformed, or
c) the appreciation of which occurred, even if only in part, through a thing constituting direct proceeds of criminal activity. Writings
Writings mean data, sound and image recordings, depictions and other representations, unless otherwise provided by individual provisions of the criminal law. Computer system
A computer system means a device or a group of interconnected or associated devices, one or more of which performs automatic data processing on the basis of a program. A computer system also means data stored, processed, retrieved or transmitted by this device or group of devices for the purpose of its or their operation, use, protection and maintenance. Determination of the amount of damage
When determining the amount of damage, the price at which the thing which was the object of the attack is usually sold at the time and place of the act shall be taken as a basis. If the amount of damage cannot be determined in this way, the reasonably incurred costs of acquiring the same or similar thing or restoring the thing to its previous state shall be taken as a basis. Limits of the amount of damage, benefit, costs of remedying environmental damage and value of a thing
(1) For the purposes of this Act, it is understood
a) as damage not insignificant, damage reaching an amount of at least CZK 10,000,
b) as damage not small, damage reaching an amount of at least CZK 50,000,
c) as greater damage, damage reaching an amount of at least CZK 100,000,
d) as substantial damage, damage reaching an amount of at least CZK 1,000,000 and
e) as damage of large extent, damage reaching an amount of at least CZK 10,000,000. (2) The amounts referred to in paragraph 1 shall apply mutatis mutandis to the determination of the amount of benefit, costs of remedying the consequences of environmental damage and the value of a thing. Calculation of time
Where this Act associates some effect with the expiry of a certain period, the day on which the event determining its beginning occurred shall not be included in it. SPECIAL PART
CRIMINAL OFFENCES AGAINST LIFE AND HEALTH
Criminal offences against life
Murder
(1) Whoever intentionally kills another shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to eighteen years. (2) Whoever intentionally kills another with deliberation or after prior consideration shall be punished by imprisonment for twelve to twenty years. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for fifteen to twenty years or by an exceptional sentence if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2
c) on a child under fifteen years of age,
d) on an official person in the exercise or for the exercise of his authority,
e) on a witness, expert or interpreter in connection with the performance of their duty,
f) on a healthcare worker in the exercise of a healthcare occupation or profession aimed at saving life or protecting health, or on another who was fulfilling his similar duty in the protection of life, health or property arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law,
g) on another for his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion,
i) in a particularly cruel or tormenting manner, or
j) with the intention of obtaining a pecuniary benefit for himself or for another or in an effort to conceal or facilitate another criminal offence or from another reprehensible motive. Manslaughter
(1) Whoever intentionally kills another in strong agitation from fear, fright, confusion or other excusable emotion or as a result of the injured person's prior reprehensible conduct shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to fifteen years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
c) on a child under fifteen years of age. Killing of a newborn child by the mother
A mother who, in agitation caused by childbirth, intentionally kills her newborn child during or immediately after childbirth shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years. Causing death by negligence
(1) Whoever causes the death of another by negligence shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or by prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to six years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 because he violated an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 because he grossly violated laws on environmental protection or laws on occupational safety or traffic safety or hygiene laws. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he causes the death of at least two persons by the act referred to in paragraph 3. Participation in suicide
(1) Whoever induces another to commit suicide or assists another in committing suicide shall be punished, if at least an attempt at suicide occurred, by imprisonment for up to three years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a child or on a pregnant woman. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a child under fifteen years of age or on a person suffering from a mental disorder. Criminal offences against health
Serious bodily harm
(1) Whoever intentionally causes serious bodily harm to another shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
c) on a child under fifteen years of age,
d) on a witness, expert or interpreter in connection with the performance of their duty,
e) on a healthcare worker in the exercise of a healthcare occupation or profession aimed at saving life or protecting health, or on another who was fulfilling his similar duty in the protection of life, health or property arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law,
f) on another for his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion,
g) repeatedly or after he committed another particularly serious felony connected with intentionally causing serious bodily harm or death or its attempt, or
(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years if he causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1. Bodily harm
(1) Whoever intentionally causes bodily harm to another shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
b) on a child under fifteen years of age,
c) on a witness, expert or interpreter in connection with the performance of their duty,
d) on a healthcare worker in the exercise of a healthcare occupation or profession aimed at saving life or protecting health, or on another who was fulfilling his similar duty in the protection of life, health or property arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law, or
e) on another for his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if he causes serious bodily harm by the act referred to in paragraph 1. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to ten years if he causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1. Bodily harm from an excusable motive
(1) Whoever intentionally causes bodily harm to another in strong agitation from fear, fright, confusion or other excusable emotion or as a result of the injured person's prior reprehensible conduct shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years if he causes serious bodily harm by the act referred to in paragraph 1. (3) Whoever intentionally causes serious bodily harm to another in strong agitation from fear, fright, confusion or other excusable emotion or as a result of the injured person's prior reprehensible conduct shall be punished by imprisonment for up to four years. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to six years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 3 on two or more persons,
b) he commits such an act on a pregnant woman, or
c) he commits such an act on a child under fifteen years of age. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if he causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 3. Serious bodily harm by negligence
(1) Whoever causes serious bodily harm to another by negligence shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or by prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to four years or by a monetary sentence if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 because he violated an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law. (3) Whoever causes serious bodily harm to at least two persons by negligence because he grossly violated laws on environmental protection or laws on occupational safety or traffic safety or hygiene laws shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years. Bodily harm by negligence
(1) Whoever causes bodily harm to another by negligence by violating an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year or by prohibition of activity. (2) Whoever causes bodily harm to at least two persons by negligence because he grossly violated laws on environmental protection or laws on occupational safety or traffic safety or hygiene laws shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. Criminal offences endangering life or health
Torture and other inhuman and cruel treatment
(1) Whoever, by torture or other inhuman and cruel treatment in connection with the exercise of authority of a state administration body, territorial self-government, court or other public authority, causes physical or mental suffering to another shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as an official person,
b) he commits such an act on a witness, expert or interpreter in connection with the performance of their duty,
c) he commits such an act on another for his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion,
d) he commits such an act with at least two persons, or
e) he commits such an act repeatedly. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a pregnant woman,
b) he commits such an act on a child under fifteen years of age,
c) he commits such an act in a particularly cruel or tormenting manner, or
d) he causes serious bodily harm by such an act.(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to eighteen years if he causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1. Failure to Provide Assistance
(1) Whoever fails to provide necessary assistance to a person who is in danger of death or shows signs of serious health disorder or other serious illness, although he can do so without danger to himself or another, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) Whoever fails to provide necessary assistance to a person who is in danger of death or shows signs of serious health disorder or serious illness, although he is obliged to provide such assistance by the nature of his employment, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or prohibition of activity. Failure to Provide Assistance by a Driver of a Vehicle
A driver of a vehicle who, after a traffic accident in which he was involved, fails to provide necessary assistance to a person who suffered injury to health in the accident, although he can do so without danger to himself or another, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years or prohibition of activity. Spreading Contagious Human Disease
(1) Whoever intentionally causes or increases the danger of introduction or spread of a contagious disease in humans shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war, during a natural disaster or other event seriously threatening the life or health of people, public order or property,
c) he breaches by such an act an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law, or
d) he causes by such an act serious injury to health. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 serious injury to health of at least two persons or death. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 the death of at least two persons. Spreading Contagious Human Disease by Negligence
(1) Whoever by negligence causes or increases the danger of introduction or spread of a contagious disease in humans shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war, during a natural disaster or other event seriously threatening the life or health of people, public order or property,
b) he breaches by such an act an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law, or
c) he causes by such an act serious injury to health. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to six years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death, or
b) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 2(b) serious injury to health. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 3 because he grossly breached laws for the protection of public health. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 the death of at least two persons because he grossly breached regulations for the protection of public health. Common Provision
The Government shall determine by regulation what is considered to be contagious human diseases. Endangerment by Venereal Disease
Whoever exposes another, even by negligence, to the danger of infection with a venereal disease shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. Endangering Health with Defective Foodstuffs and Other Items
(1) Whoever, in breach of another legal regulation, has for sale or manufactures for this purpose or procures for himself or another intentionally foodstuffs or other items whose consumption or use for the usual purpose is dangerous to human health shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war, during a natural disaster or other event seriously threatening the life or health of people, public order or property,
c) he breaches by such an act an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law, or
d) he causes by such an act serious injury to health. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 serious injury to health of at least two persons or death. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 the death of at least two persons. Endangering Health with Defective Foodstuffs and Other Items by Negligence
(1) Whoever, in breach of another legal regulation, has for sale or manufactures for this purpose or procures for himself or another by negligence foodstuffs or other items whose consumption or use for the usual purpose is dangerous to human health shall be punished by imprisonment for up to six months, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war, during a natural disaster or other event seriously threatening the life or health of people, public order or property,
b) he breaches by such an act an important duty arising from his employment, profession, position or function or imposed on him by law, or
c) he causes by such an act serious injury to health. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death, or
b) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 2(b) serious injury to health. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 3(a) because he grossly breached hygienic or other laws relating to such foodstuffs or items. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 the death of at least two persons because he grossly breached hygienic or other laws relating to such foodstuffs or items. Affray
(1) Whoever intentionally endangers the life or health of another by participating in an affray shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years or a fine if serious injury to health is caused to another during the act referred to in paragraph 1. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if death is caused to another during the act referred to in paragraph 1. Criminal Offences Against Pregnancy of a Woman
Unlawful Termination of Pregnancy Without Consent of the Pregnant Woman
(1) Whoever artificially terminates the pregnancy of a pregnant woman without her consent shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a woman under eighteen years of age,
b) he commits such an act using violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm,
c) he commits such an act by abusing the distress or dependence of the pregnant woman,
d) he commits such an act repeatedly, or
e) he causes by such an act serious injury to health. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 serious injury to health of at least two persons or death. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 the death of at least two persons. Unlawful Termination of Pregnancy With Consent of the Pregnant Woman
(1) Whoever, with the consent of a pregnant woman, artificially terminates her pregnancy otherwise than in a manner permissible under the law on artificial termination of pregnancy shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years or prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a woman under eighteen years of age,
b) he obtains by such an act for himself or another substantial benefit,
c) he commits such an act systematically, or
d) he causes by such an act serious injury to health. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 serious injury to health of at least two persons or death. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 the death of at least two persons. Assisting a Pregnant Woman in Artificial Termination of Pregnancy
(1) Whoever assists a pregnant woman so that
a) she herself artificially terminates her pregnancy, or
b) she requests another or allows another to artificially terminate her pregnancy otherwise than in a manner permissible under the law on artificial termination of pregnancy,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 towards a woman under eighteen years of age, or
b) he contributes by such an act to serious injury to health of the pregnant woman. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to six years if he contributes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 to the death of the pregnant woman. Inducing a Pregnant Woman to Artificial Termination of Pregnancy
(1) Whoever induces a pregnant woman so that
a) she herself artificially terminates her pregnancy, or
b) she requests another or allows another to artificially terminate her pregnancy otherwise than in a manner permissible under the law on artificial termination of pregnancy,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 towards a woman under eighteen years of age,
b) he commits such an act by abusing the distress or dependence of the pregnant woman, or
c) he contributes by such an act to serious injury to health of the pregnant woman. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to six years if he contributes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 to the death of the pregnant woman. Common Provision
A pregnant woman who herself artificially terminates her pregnancy or requests another to do so or allows him to do so shall not be criminally liable for such an act, not even under the provisions on instigators and accomplices. Criminal Offences Related to Unauthorised Handling of Human Tissues and Organs, Human Embryo and Human Genome
Unauthorised Removal of Tissues and Organs
(1) Whoever, in breach of another legal regulation, performs on another a removal of tissue, cell or organ from his body shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever, in breach of another legal regulation, procures, intermediates, offers, imports, exports or transports for himself or another removed human tissue, cell or removed human organ from the body of a living person, or otherwise handles such tissue, cell or organ. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 on a child,
b) he commits such an act using violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm,
c) he commits such an act by abusing distress or dependence,
d) he commits such an act on at least two persons,
e) he commits such an act repeatedly,
f) he commits such an act as a member of an organised group,
g) he causes by such an act serious injury to health, or
h) he obtains by such an act for himself or another substantial benefit. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 on a child under fifteen years of age,
b) he commits such an act in connection with an organised group operating in more than one state,
c) he causes by such an act death, or
d) he obtains by such an act for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Unlawful Handling of Tissues and Organs
(1) Whoever, in breach of another legal regulation, performs a removal of tissue, cell or organ from the body of a dead person shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or prohibition of activity. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever handles removed human tissue, cell or removed human organ in breach of another legal regulation with the intention of enriching himself or another. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 repeatedly,
b) he commits such an act as a member of an organised group, or
c) he obtains by such an act for himself or another substantial benefit. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years or forfeiture of property if
a) he obtains by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 for himself or another benefit on a large scale, or
b) he commits such an act in connection with an organised group operating in more than one state. Removal of Tissue, Organ and Performance of Transplantation for Remuneration
(1) Whoever, in breach of another legal regulation, offers, promises or provides to another or for another remuneration for the removal of tissue or organ from his body or for the performance of a transplantation shall be punished by imprisonment for up to five years. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever, himself or through another, in connection with the removal of tissue or organ or with the performance of a transplantation, requests, accepts or has promised for himself or another remuneration in breach of another legal regulation. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organised group,
b) serious injury to health occurs in connection with such an act, or
c) such an act is committed towards a child. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 in connection with an organised group operating in more than one state,
b) death occurs in connection with such an act, or
c) he commits such an act towards a child under fifteen years of age. Unlawful Handling of Human Embryo and Human Genome
(1) Whoever, in breach of another legal regulation,
a) uses for research a human embryo or a larger quantity of human embryonic stem cells or their lines,
b) imports or exports a human embryo or a larger quantity of human embryonic stem cells or their lines, or
c) transfers human genome into cells of another animal species or vice versa,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if
a) he performs interventions aimed at creating a human embryo for a purpose other than for transfer into a female organism,
b) he transfers a created human embryo into the womb of another animal species, or
c) during research on human embryonic stem cells he performs manipulations with these cells aimed at creating a new human individual (reproductive cloning). (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act repeatedly, or
c) he obtains by such an act for himself or another substantial benefit. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 in connection with an organised group operating in more than one state, or
b) he obtains by such an act for himself or another benefit on a large scale. CRIMINAL OFFENCES AGAINST FREEDOM AND RIGHTS TO PROTECTION OF PERSONALITY, PRIVACY AND SECRECY OF CORRESPONDENCE
Criminal Offences Against Freedom
Trafficking in Human Beings
(1) Whoever induces, procures, hires, lures, entices, transports, conceals, harbours, receives or hands over a child so that it may be used
a) by another for sexual intercourse or other forms of sexual abuse or harassment or for the production of pornographic work,
b) by another for the removal of tissue, cell or organ from its body,
c) for service in armed forces,
d) for slavery or serfdom, or
e) for forced labour or other forms of exploitation, or
whoever profits from such conduct,
shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever, using violence, threat of violence or other serious harm or deceit or by abusing his error, distress or dependence, induces, procures, hires, lures, entices, transports, conceals, harbours, receives or hands over a person other than that referred to in paragraph 1 so that he may be used
a) by another for sexual intercourse or other forms of sexual abuse or harassment or for the production of pornographic work,
b) by another for the removal of tissue, cell or organ from his body,
c) for service in armed forces,
d) for slavery or serfdom, or
e) for forced labour or other forms of exploitation, or
whoever profits from such conduct. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organised group,
b) he exposes another by such an act to the danger of serious injury to health or death,
c) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit, or
d) he commits such an act with the intention that another be used for prostitution. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years or forfeiture of property if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 serious injury to health,
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale, or
c) he commits such an act in connection with an organised group operating in more than one state. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to eighteen years or forfeiture of property if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 death. Entrusting a Child into the Power of Another
(1) Whoever, for remuneration, entrusts a child into the power of another for the purpose of adoption or for another similar purpose shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years or forfeiture of property if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he causes by such an act serious injury to health,
c) he commits such an act repeatedly, or
d) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years or forfeiture of property if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death,
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale, or
c) he commits such an act in connection with an organised group operating in more than one state. Deprivation of Personal Freedom
(1) Whoever unlawfully imprisons another or otherwise deprives him of personal freedom shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act on another because of his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion,
c) he causes by such an act physical or psychological suffering,
d) he causes by such an act serious injury to health, or
e) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death, or
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Restriction of Personal Freedom
(1) Whoever unlawfully prevents another from exercising personal freedom shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 with the intention of facilitating another criminal offence. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act on another because of his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion,
c) he causes by such an act physical or psychological suffering,
d) he causes by such an act serious injury to health, or
e) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death, or
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Abduction
(1) Whoever, by deceit, violence or threat of violence or other harm, abducts another into the Czech Republic or into another state, or induces him to go to the Czech Republic or to another state, or prevents him from returning from the Czech Republic or from another state, shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act on another because of his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion,
c) he causes by such an act physical or psychological suffering,
d) he causes by such an act serious injury to health, or
e) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death, or
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Robbery
(1) Whoever uses violence or threat of imminent violence against another with the intention of appropriating another's property shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he causes by such an act serious injury to health,
c) he causes by such an act substantial damage, or
d) he commits such an act with the intention of enabling or facilitating the commission of a terrorist criminal offence, the criminal offence of financing terrorism (Section 312d) or threatening a terrorist criminal offence (Section 312f). (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 damage on a large scale. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to eighteen years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death. Taking Hostages
(1) Whoever takes a hostage and threatens to kill him or to cause him injury to health or other serious harm, with the aim of forcing another to do, omit or tolerate something, shall be punished by imprisonment for two to ten years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) a child is a hostage in such an act,
c) more persons are hostages in such an act,
d) he causes by such an act serious injury to health, or
e) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years if he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to eighteen years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death. Extortion
(1) Whoever forces another by violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm to do, omit or tolerate something shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to four years or a fine. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two to eight years if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act with at least two persons,
c) he commits such an act with a weapon,
d) he causes by such an act substantial damage,
e) he commits such an act on a pregnant woman,
f) he commits such an act on a witness, expert or interpreter in connection with the performance of their duty, or
g) he commits such an act on another because of his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if
a) he causes by such an act serious injury to health,
b) he commits such an act with the intention of enabling or facilitating the commission of a terrorist criminal offence, the criminal offence of financing terrorism (Section 312d) or threatening a terrorist criminal offence (Section 312f), or
c) he causes by such an act damage on a large scale. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to sixteen years if he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 death. Restriction of Freedom of Religion
(1) Whoever, by violence, threat of violence or threat of other harm,
a) forces another to participate in a religious act,
b) unlawfully prevents another from such participation, or
c) otherwise prevents another from exercising freedom of religion,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years or a fine if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
a) on at least three persons, or
Oppression
(1) Whoever forces another, by abusing his distress or dependence, to do, omit or tolerate something shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year or prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 substantial damage, or
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years or a fine if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 damage on a large scale, or
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Violation of Domiciliary Freedom
(1) Whoever unlawfully enters the dwelling of another or unlawfully remains there shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if he uses violence or threat of imminent violence during the act referred to in paragraph 1 or overcomes an obstacle whose purpose is to prevent entry. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years or a fine if he uses violence or threat of imminent violence during the act referred to in paragraph 1 and commits such an act with a weapon or with at least two persons. Violation of Freedom of Association and Assembly
(1) Whoever restricts another in the exercise of his right of association or assembly by violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or prohibition of activity. (2) Whoever, in connection with an assembly that is subject to notification obligation, opposes by violence or threat of imminent violence the order measures of the convener or designated stewards of such assembly shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. Criminal Offences Against Rights to Protection of Personality, Privacy and Secrecy of Correspondence
Unauthorised Handling of Personal Data
(1) Whoever, even by negligence, unlawfully publishes, communicates, makes accessible, otherwise processes or appropriates personal data that were collected about another in connection with the exercise of public authority, and thereby causes serious harm to the rights or legitimate interests of the person to whom the personal data relate, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or prohibition of activity. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever, even by negligence, breaches a duty of confidentiality imposed or recognised by the state by unlawfully publishing, communicating or making accessible to a third person personal data obtained in connection with the exercise of his profession, employment or function, and thereby causes serious harm to the rights or legitimate interests of the person to whom the personal data relate. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years, a fine or prohibition of activity if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act by press, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means,
c) he causes by such an act substantial damage, or
d) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to eight years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 damage on a large scale, or
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Damage to Rights of Others
(1) Whoever causes another serious harm to rights by
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 substantial harm to the rights of another,
b) he obtains by such an act for himself or another substantial benefit, or
c) he poses during such an act as an official person. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1 harm to the rights of another on a large scale, or
b) he obtains by such an act for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Breach of Secrecy of Transmitted Messages
(1) Whoever intentionally breaches the secrecy of
a) a sealed letter or other document in the provision of postal service or transported by another transport service or transport facility,
b) a data, text, voice, sound or image message sent via an electronic communications network and attributable to an identified subscriber or user who receives the message, or
c) a non-public transmission of data to, from or within a computer system, including electromagnetic emissions from a computer system transmitting such data,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or prohibition of activity. (2) The same punishment shall be imposed on whoever, with the intention of causing damage to another or obtaining for himself or another unauthorised benefit,
a) discloses a secret of which he learned from a document, telegram, telephone conversation or transmission via an electronic communications network that was not intended for him, or
b) uses such a secret. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years or prohibition of activity if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act from a reprehensible motive,
c) he causes by such an act substantial damage, or
d) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years or a fine if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as an official person,
b) he causes by such an act damage on a large scale, or
c) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. (5) An employee of a postal service provider, telecommunications service or computer system or anyone else performing communication activities who
a) commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2,
b) intentionally enables another to commit such an act, or
c) alters or suppresses a document contained in a postal consignment or transported by a transport facility or a message submitted by non-public data transmission, by telephone, telegraph or other similar means,
shall be punished by imprisonment for one to five years, a fine or prohibition of activity. (6) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three to ten years if
a) he causes by the act referred to in paragraph 5 damage on a large scale, or
b) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another benefit on a large scale. Breach of Secrecy of Documents and Other Records Kept in Private
(1) Whoever unlawfully breaches the secrecy of a document or other writing, photograph, film or other recording, data stored in a computer system or on an information carrier or other document kept in the private sphere of another by publishing it, making it accessible to a third person or using it in another manner shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing if he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 with the intention of obtaining for himself or another property or other benefit, causing damage or other serious harm to another, or threatening his social reputation. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years or a fine if
a) he commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) he commits such an act towards another because of his actual or supposed race, membership of an ethnic group, nationality, political conviction, religion or because he is actually or supposedly without religion,
c) he causes by such an act substantial damage, or
d) he commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or another substantial benefit.(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years,
a) if he or she causes damage on a large scale by the act referred to in paragraph 1, or
b) if he or she commits such an act with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a benefit on a large scale. Defamation
(1) Whoever communicates about another an untrue statement that is capable of significantly endangering his or her reputation among fellow citizens, in particular of harming him or her in employment, disrupting his or her family relations or causing him or her other serious harm, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or by prohibition of activity if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 by print, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means. CRIMINAL OFFENCES AGAINST HUMAN DIGNITY IN THE SEXUAL SPHERE
Rape
(1) Whoever compels another by violence or threat of violence or threat of other serious harm to sexual intercourse, or
whoever abuses his or her defencelessness for such an act,
shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to ten years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
a) by coitus or other sexual intercourse performed in a manner comparable to coitus,
(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a child under fifteen years of age,
b) if he or she commits such an act on a person in the execution of custody, imprisonment, protective treatment, security detention, protective or institutional education or in another place where personal freedom is restricted, or
c) if he or she causes serious harm to health by such an act. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to eighteen years if he or she causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1. Sexual coercion
(1) Whoever compels another by violence, threat of violence or threat of other serious harm to sexual self-gratification, to undressing or other comparable behaviour, or
whoever induces another to such behaviour by abusing his or her defencelessness,
shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to four years or by prohibition of activity. (2) The offender shall be punished in the same manner if he or she induces another to sexual intercourse, sexual self-gratification, undressing or other comparable behaviour by abusing his or her dependence or his or her position and the trustworthiness or influence arising therefrom. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2
(4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 with a weapon,
b) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 on a person in the execution of custody, imprisonment, protective treatment, security detention, protective or institutional education or in another place where personal freedom is restricted, or
c) if he or she commits such an act as a member of an organised group. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a child under fifteen years of age, or
b) if he or she causes serious harm to health by such an act. (6) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to sixteen years if he or she causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2. Sexual abuse
(1) Whoever performs coitus with a child under fifteen years of age or whoever sexually abuses him or her in another manner shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to eight years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to ten years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 on a child under fifteen years of age entrusted to his or her supervision, abusing his or her dependence or his or her position and the trustworthiness or influence arising therefrom. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he or she causes serious harm to health by the act referred to in paragraph 1. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for ten to eighteen years if he or she causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1. Coitus between relatives
Whoever performs coitus with a relative in direct line or with a sibling shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years. Procuring
(1) Whoever induces, procures, hires, entices or seduces another to engage in prostitution, or
whoever profits from prostitution engaged in by another,
shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to four years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
a) with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a substantial benefit, or
b) as a member of an organised group. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years or forfeiture of property if he or she causes serious harm to health by the act referred to in paragraph 1. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for eight to fifteen years or forfeiture of property if he or she causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1. Prostitution endangering the moral development of children
(1) Whoever engages in prostitution in the vicinity of a school, educational or other similar facility or place that is reserved or designated for the stay or visit of children shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) Whoever organises, guards or otherwise ensures the engagement in prostitution in the vicinity of a school, educational or other similar facility or place that is reserved or designated for the stay or visit of children shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years or forfeiture of property if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2
a) in at least two such places, or
Dissemination of pornography
(1) Whoever produces, imports, exports, transports, offers, makes publicly accessible, mediates, puts into circulation, sells or otherwise procures for another a photographic, film, computer, electronic or other pornographic work in which violence or disrespect for a person is manifested, or which describes, depicts or otherwise portrays sexual intercourse with an animal, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) Whoever
a) offers, hands over or makes accessible a written, photographic, film, computer, electronic or other pornographic work to a child, or
b) displays or otherwise makes accessible such work in a place accessible to children,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2
a) as a member of an organised group,
b) by print, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means, or
c) with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a substantial benefit. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2
a) as a member of an organised group operating in more than one state, or
b) with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a benefit on a large scale. Production and other handling of child pornography
(1) Whoever possesses a photographic, film, computer, electronic or other pornographic work that depicts or otherwise uses a child or a person who appears to be a child shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) Whoever obtains access to child pornography by means of information or communication technology shall be punished in the same manner. (3) Whoever produces, imports, exports, transports, offers, makes publicly accessible, mediates, puts into circulation, sells or otherwise procures for another a photographic, film, computer, electronic or other pornographic work that depicts or otherwise uses a child or a person who appears to be a child, or
whoever profits from such pornographic work,
shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to six years or forfeiture of property if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 3
a) as a member of an organised group,
b) by print, film, radio, television, publicly accessible computer network or other similarly effective means, or
c) with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a substantial benefit. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to eight years or forfeiture of property if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 3
a) as a member of an organised group operating in more than one state, or
b) with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a benefit on a large scale. Abuse of a child for the production of pornography
(1) Whoever induces, procures, hires, entices, seduces or abuses a child for the production of a pornographic work or profits from the participation of a child in such a pornographic work shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to six years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
a) as a member of an organised group, or
b) with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a substantial benefit. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to eight years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
a) as a member of an organised group operating in more than one state, or
b) with the intention of obtaining for himself or herself or for another a benefit on a large scale. Participation in a pornographic performance
Whoever participates in a pornographic performance or other similar show in which a child performs shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. Establishing unlawful contact with a child
Whoever proposes a meeting to a child under fifteen years of age with the intention of committing a criminal offence under Section 187(1), Sections 192, 193, Section 202(3) or another sexually motivated criminal offence shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. CRIMINAL OFFENCES AGAINST THE FAMILY AND CHILDREN
Bigamy
(1) Whoever enters into another marriage during the subsistence of his or her marriage shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) Whoever enters into marriage with a person who is already in another marriage shall be punished in the same manner. Abandonment of a child or entrusted person
(1) Whoever abandons a child or another person whom he or she has a duty to care for and who cannot obtain help for himself or herself, and thereby exposes him or her to danger of death or harm to health, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1
(3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years if he or she causes serious harm to health by the act referred to in paragraph 1. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to ten years if he or she causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1. Neglect of compulsory maintenance
(1) Whoever fails to fulfil, even through negligence, his or her statutory duty to maintain or provide for another for a period longer than four months shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. (2) Whoever intentionally evades the fulfilment of his or her statutory duty to maintain or provide for another for a period longer than four months shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if he or she exposes the entitled person to danger of hardship by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2. Special provision on punishment
The court may impose on the offender of the criminal offence of neglect of compulsory maintenance (Section 196), as appropriate restrictions and appropriate obligations referred to in Section 48(4), also an appropriate restriction to refrain from driving motor vehicles. The court shall impose this appropriate restriction in particular where there is reasonable apprehension that the obligation to pay the maintenance owed will be frustrated or made more difficult. Special provision on effective remorse
Criminal liability for the criminal offence of neglect of compulsory maintenance (Section 196) shall cease if the criminal offence did not have permanently adverse consequences and the offender subsequently fulfilled his or her obligation before the court of first instance began to pronounce the judgment. Ill-treatment of an entrusted person
(1) Whoever ill-treats a person who is in his or her care or upbringing shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 in a particularly cruel or tormenting manner,
b) if he or she causes serious harm to health by such an act,
c) if he or she commits such an act on at least two persons, or
d) if he or she commits such an act over a longer period. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he or she causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1
a) serious harm to health to at least two persons, or
Ill-treatment of a person living in a common dwelling
(1) Whoever ill-treats a close person or another person living with him or her in a common dwelling shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to four years. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 in a particularly cruel or tormenting manner,
b) if he or she causes serious harm to health by such an act,
c) if he or she commits such an act on at least two persons, or
d) if he or she commits such an act over a longer period. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to twelve years if he or she causes by the act referred to in paragraph 1
a) serious harm to health to at least two persons, or
Abduction of a child and a person afflicted with a mental disorder
(1) Whoever removes a child or a person afflicted with a mental disorder from the care of the person who has a duty to care for them under another legal regulation or under an official decision shall be punished by imprisonment for up to three years or a fine. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 with the intention of procuring for himself or herself or for another a property benefit, or
b) if he or she endangers the moral development of the abducted person by such an act. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
b) if he or she causes serious harm to health by the act referred to in paragraph 1, or
c) if he or she obtains for himself or herself or for another a substantial benefit by such an act. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for three years to ten years,
a) if he or she causes death by the act referred to in paragraph 1, or
b) if he or she obtains for himself or herself or for another a benefit on a large scale by such an act. Endangering the upbringing of a child
(1) Whoever, even through negligence, endangers the intellectual, emotional or moral development of a child by
a) inducing him or her to lead an idle or immoral life,
b) enabling him or her to lead an idle or immoral life,
c) enabling him or her to procure for himself or herself or for another means by criminal activity or other reprehensible manner, or
d) seriously breaching his or her duty to care for him or her or another important duty arising from parental responsibility,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years. (2) Whoever enables, even through negligence, a child to play on a gaming machine that is equipped with a technical device that influences the result of the game and that provides the possibility of a monetary win shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year, a fine or prohibition of activity. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 from a reprehensible motive,
b) if he or she continues to commit such an act over a longer period,
c) if he or she commits such an act repeatedly, or
d) if he or she obtains for himself or herself or for another a substantial benefit by such an act. Inducement to sexual intercourse
(1) Whoever offers, promises or provides to a child or to another for sexual intercourse with a child, sexual self-gratification of a child, his or her undressing or other comparable behaviour for the purpose of sexual satisfaction a payment, advantage or benefit shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years or a fine. (2) Whoever performs sexual intercourse with a child or participates in his or her sexual self-gratification, undressing or other comparable behaviour for the purpose of his or her own sexual satisfaction, if a payment, advantage or benefit was offered, promised or provided to that child or to another for it, shall be punished in the same manner. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to five years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 on a child under fifteen years of age,
b) if he or she commits such an act from a reprehensible motive,
c) if he or she continues to commit such an act over a longer period, or
d) if he or she commits such an act repeatedly. Impunity of a child
A child who requests or accepts for sexual intercourse with him or her, his or her sexual self-gratification, undressing or other comparable behaviour a payment or other advantage or benefit is not punishable for such an act, not even under the provisions on instigator or accomplice. Serving alcohol to a child
Whoever to a greater extent or repeatedly sells, serves or provides alcohol to a child shall be punished by imprisonment for up to one year. CRIMINAL OFFENCES AGAINST PROPERTY
Theft
(1) Whoever appropriates another's thing by taking possession of it, and
a) thereby causes damage not insignificant to another's property,
c) immediately after the act attempts to retain the thing by violence or threat of immediate violence,
d) commits the act on a thing that another has on his or her person or with him or her, or
e) commits the act in a territory in which evacuation of persons is being carried out or has been carried out,
shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) Whoever appropriates another's thing by taking possession of it, and was convicted or punished for such an act in the last three years, shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years or a fine if he or she causes greater damage by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2 as a member of an organised group,
b) if he or she commits such an act during a state of threat to the state or during a state of war, during a natural disaster or other event seriously threatening the life or health of people, public order or property, or
c) if he or she causes substantial damage by such an act. (5) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for five to ten years,
a) if he or she causes damage on a large scale by the act referred to in paragraph 1 or 2, or
b) if he or she commits such an act with the intention of enabling or facilitating the commission of a terrorist criminal offence, the criminal offence of financing terrorism (Section 312d) or threatening a terrorist criminal offence (Section 312f). Embezzlement
(1) Whoever appropriates another's thing that was entrusted to him or her, and thereby causes damage not insignificant to another's property, shall be punished by imprisonment for up to two years, prohibition of activity or forfeiture of a thing. (2) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for six months to three years if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 and was convicted or punished for such an act in the last three years. (3) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for one year to five years or a fine if he or she causes greater damage by the act referred to in paragraph 1. (4) The offender shall be punished by imprisonment for two years to eight years,
a) if he or she commits the act referred to in paragraph 1 as a member of an organised group,
